, which means that
the step also matches
\leq
in case
x_{n+1}
is a terminated event.

*

Case
v=false
.
By (
18
) and Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::helped-are-writers}}
,
we have
z\in\mathcal{W}_{{pt}_{k}}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {{pt}_{k}}\ {v^{\prime}}
for some

k\in\overline{u}
and
v^{\prime}\neq{exp}_{k}
.

By a similar argument as in the previous case,

H_{n}({pt}_{k})=v^{\prime}
, where
v^{\prime}\neq{exp}_{k}
.

Define
H_{n+1}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}H_{n}
.
Hence,
H_{n}\xrightarrow{\textit{mcas}(\overline{u})\ \left\langle false\right\rangle}H_{n+1}
is a valid
step corresponding to a failing
mcas
step.

In case
x_{n+1}\in T
, Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::fin-predicate}}

also concludes that
false=v={x_{n+1}}.\textit{out}
, which means that
the step also matches
\leq
in case
x_{n+1}
is a terminated event.

–

Case
x_{n+1}=\textit{malloc}(v)
.
Identical to the case for
ralloc
in Lemma
A.12
.

–

Case
x_{n+1}=\textit{mwrite}(q,m)
.
Identical to the case for
rwrite
in Lemma
A.12
.

–

Case
x_{n+1}=\textit{mread}(q)
.
Identical to the case for
rread
in Lemma
A.12
.

∎

Lemma A.18

(Sequential Soundness)
.

Suppose hypotheses
A.15
.
The linear order
\leq
of Lemma
A.8
is sequentially sound.

Proof.

If
\overline{T}=\emptyset
, then the empty path matches
\leq
.
If
\overline{T}\neq\emptyset
, then Lemma
A.17

applied with
n=|\overline{T}|
ensures the existence of a matching path
for
\leq
.
∎

Theorem A.2
.

Given an implementation of the MCAS, suppose that for any set of
abstract events
Ev
generated from an arbitrary execution
history in the implementation, there are relations

\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
,

\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}
such that

V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}})
is valid. Then, the
implementation is linearizable.

Proof.

Let
Ev
be a set of events generated from an arbitrary
execution history in the implementation.
From the hypothesis,
V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}})
is valid
for some
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
and

\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}
.
Let
\leq
be the linear order of Lemma

A.8
. Then, we take the general
visibility relation
\prec
and
\leq
to be the
relations required by the definition of linearizability.
By construction,
\leq
respects both
\prec
and

\sqsubset
. Also,

\leq
is sequentially sound by Lemma

A.18
.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Validity for RDCSS and helping MCAS

This section focuses on proving the validity of
V^{\mu}

(
\mu\in\{\textsc{R},\textsc{M}\}
) as defined in

A.6
for
\mu=\textsc{R}
,
and in
A.12
for
\mu=\textsc{M}
,
under the assumption that
V^{\mu}
can be implemented
by spans (i.e., the arrow stating that the span axioms imply the visibility axioms in Figure
1
).
Appendix
C
will then focus on proving that

V^{\mu}
is implemented by spans.

This section makes use of all concepts in Section
5.2
but
augments the notions of span structure and implementation by span
structure to include allocs.

Definition B.1

(Span Structure)
.

A
span structure

(\mathcal{S}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)
consists
of:

•

For every pointer
p
, a set
\mathcal{S}_{p}\subseteq\textsc{Rep}\times\textsc{Rep}
, called the
spans
accessing
p
.

•

For every pointer
p
, a set
\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{p}
, called the
successful write spans
into
p
.

•

For every pointer
p
, a set
\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
, called the
alloc spans
creating
p
.

•

A function
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket:\textsc{Ev}\rightarrow(\mathcal{P}(\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p})\times(\textsc{Val}\cup\{\bot\}))
,
called the
event denotation
, written
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
for event
x
. Here,
\mathcal{P}(\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p})
denotes the power set of
\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
.

Each span in
\mathcal{S}_{p}
is a pair of rep events. We denote as

f
(“first”) and
l
(“last”)
the standard pair projection functions. We write spans of the form

(a,a)
as
(a)
.

Definition B.2

(Implementation by a span structure)
.

Given arbitrary relations

\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
,

\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}
, we say that

V(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})=(\mathcal{W}_{p},\mathcal{A}_{p},\mathcal{Q})
is
implemented by span
structure

(\mathcal{S}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)
if all
the span axioms in Figure
12
are
satisfied. We also say that

V(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})=(\mathcal{W}_{p},\mathcal{A}_{p},\mathcal{Q})
is
implemented by
spans
if there exists some span structure that implements it.

Span returns-before relation

{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{l({b})}<^{R}{f({c})}

Set of spans in a denotation

\mathcal{S}(x)\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\pi_{1}({\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket})

Denotation output

o({x})\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\pi_{2}({\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket})

(a)

Defined notions for span axioms.

(
S_{1}
) Disjointness

(
S_{2}
) Bunching

b,c\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\implies({{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}}\vee{{c}\sqsubset^{S}{b}}\vee{b=c})

b,c\in\mathcal{S}(x)\implies{f({b})}\leq^{R}{l({c})}

(
S_{3}
) Adequacy

x\in T\implies o({x})={x}.\textit{out}\wedge\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset

(b)

Key Axioms

(
S_{4}
)

{f({b})}\leq^{R}{l({b})}

(
S_{5}
)

\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}(y)\neq\emptyset\implies x=y

(
S_{6}
)

b\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\implies\exists x.\ b\in\mathcal{S}(x)

(
S_{7}
)

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset\implies o({x})\neq\bot\wedge\mathcal{Q}_{{x},{o({x})}}

(
S_{8}
)

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset\implies(x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\neq\emptyset)

(
S_{9}
)

(i)
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\implies{x}.\textit{start}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{f({b})}.\textit{start}

(ii)
(b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\wedge x\in T)\implies{}

\exists i\in[\leq^{R}].\ {i}.\textit{end}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{x}.\textit{end}\wedge{l({b})}\leq^{R}{i}

(
S_{10}
)

b,c\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}\implies b=c

(
S_{11}
)

b\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\implies\exists c\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}.\ {c}\sqsubseteq^{S}{b}

(
S_{12}
)

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset\implies(x\in\mathcal{A}_{p}\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}\neq\emptyset)

(c)

Structural Axioms

Figure 12
:
Span axioms for span structure
(\mathcal{S}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)
implementing
V(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})=(\mathcal{W}_{p},\mathcal{A}_{p},\mathcal{Q})
. Variables
x
,
y
range over
Ev
. Variables
b
,
c
over
\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
. Variable
p
over
pts
.

All axioms from
S_{\ref{principle::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
to
S_{\ref{principle::finished-operations-have-a-run}}

have already been explained in Section
5.2
. Here we explain the three last ones involving allocs.
Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::containment-and-uniqueness-of-alloc-blocks}}

states that alloc spans are unique.

Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::every-block-must-have-an-allocated-pointer}}

states that every span must be preceded by an alloc span that
created the pointer.

Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::allocs-have-alloc-blocks}}
states
that
p
-alloc events must contain a
p
-alloc span in their denotation.

We will prove the validity of
V^{\mu}

for the two specific separable-before (
1
)
and observation (
2
)
relations of Section
3.3
,
which we define here again.

Definition B.3

(Visibility Relations for Helping)
.

Given span structure
(\mathcal{S}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)
,
we define the pointer-indexed separable-before
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}
and
observation
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
relations for helping implementations,

\displaystyle{A}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{B}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists b\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(A),c\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(B).\ {b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}

(19)

\displaystyle{A}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{B}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists b\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(A),c\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(B).\ b=\max_{\sqsubseteq^{S}}\{d\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\mid{d}\sqsubset^{S}{c}\}

(20)

Definition B.4

(Section Hypotheses)
.

To shorten the statements of propositions from
B.1

to
B.1
, the
following hypotheses will apply,

•

Ev
is an arbitrary set of events.

•

Rep
is an arbitrary set of rep events, linearized under a given
\leq^{R}
.

•

Z\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}(\mathcal{S}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)

is an arbitrary span structure.

•

V^{\mu}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})

(for
\mu\in\{\textsc{R},\textsc{M}\}
)
is implemented by
Z
, where relations
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
,
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}

are those in Definition
B.3
and instantiated with

Z
.

The first lemmas describe basic results whose statements are
self-explanatory.

Lemma B.1
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
Relation
\sqsubset^{S}
defines a strict partial order on the set

\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
.

Proof.

Each required property follows,

•

Irreflexivity. Suppose for a contradiction that

{a}\sqsubset^{S}{a}
for some
a\in\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
.
Hence,
l({a})<^{R}f({a})
by
definition.
But by Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

{f({a})}\leq^{R}{l({a})}
.
Therefore,
{l({a})}<^{R}{l({a})}

(Contradiction).

•

Transitivity. Let
{a}\sqsubset^{S}{b}
and

{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
.

We have by definition that

l({a})<^{R}f({b})
and

l({b})<^{R}f({c})
.
But by Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

{f({b})}\leq^{R}{l({b})}
.
Therefore,
{l({a})}<^{R}{f({c})}
,
which means
{a}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
.

∎

Lemma B.2
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
Relation
\sqsubseteq^{S}
defines a partial order on the set

\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
. In addition, for every
q
,
relation
\sqsubseteq^{S}
defines a linear order on the set

\mathcal{S}_{q}
.

Proof.

By Lemma

B.1
,

\sqsubset^{S}
defines a strict partial order. It is a
standard result that the reflexive closure of a strict partial
order defines a partial order (i.e. a reflexive, transitive,
and antisymmetric relation).

Since for every
q
,
\mathcal{S}_{q}\subseteq\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}

holds, relation
\sqsubseteq^{S}
is also a partial order
on
\mathcal{S}_{q}
for every
q
.
That
\sqsubseteq^{S}
is a linear order
for
\mathcal{S}_{q}
is simply Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
.
∎

Therefore, Lemma
B.2

implies that it makes sense to take a maximum under
\sqsubseteq^{S}

in Definition
B.3
.

The next lemma states that events related under a visibility relation
must contain disjoint spans. This is a direct consequence of
the definitions of the relations in
B.3
.

Lemma B.3
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
If
{x}\prec{y}
then
\exists b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x),b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(y).\ {b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.

Proof.

The case
{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{y}
(for some
p
) implies
by definition that
{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
for some
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(x)\subseteq\mathcal{S}(x)
and
b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(y)\subseteq\mathcal{S}(y)
.

The case

{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{y}
(for some
p
),
also implies by definition that
{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
for some
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(x)\subseteq\mathcal{S}(x)
and
b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(y)\subseteq\mathcal{S}(y)
.
∎

The next lemma states that when we apply the transitive closure to
\prec
, the property
of Lemma
B.3

remains true. This is a direct consequence of the bunching axiom.

Lemma B.4
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
If
{x}\prec^{+}{y}
, then
\exists b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x),b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(y).\ {b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.

Proof.

Define the following binary relation on
Ev
,

P(w,z)\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists b_{w}\in\mathcal{S}(w),b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}(z).\ {b_{w}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}

So, we need to show
{x}\prec^{+}{y}\implies P(x,y)
.
But it suffices to show the following two properties,

•

{\prec}\subseteq P
.

•

P
is transitive.

because
\prec^{+}
is the smallest transitive
relation containing
\prec
.

Let us show the two required properties.

•

{\prec}\subseteq P
. This is Lemma
B.3
.

•

P
is transitive. By hypotheses
P(u,v)
and
P(v,z)
,
we have,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\exists b_{u}\in\mathcal{S}(u),b_{v}\in\mathcal{S}(v).\ {b_{u}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{v}}\\
\exists b_{v}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(v),b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}(z).\ {b_{v}^{\prime}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}\end{split}

(21)

We need to show
P(u,z)
. By the bunching axiom
S_{\ref{principle::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}

on the denotation
\mathcal{S}(v)
,

{f({b_{v}})}\leq^{R}{l({b_{v}^{\prime}})}
must hold. Hence,

l({b_{u}})<^{R}f({b_{v}})\leq^{R}l({b_{v}^{\prime}})<^{R}f({b_{z}})

which means
{b_{u}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}
.

∎

A direct consequence of Lemma
B.4

is the following, which states that events chosen for linearization
must have carried out some non-empty execution path.

Lemma B.5
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
If
x\in\overline{T}
then
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
.

Proof.

By definition of
\overline{T}
,
x\mathrel{{\prec}^{*}}y

for some
y\in T
.

•

Case
x=y
. Hence,
x\in T
. By Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::finished-operations-have-a-run}}
,

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
must hold.

•

Case
x\neq y
. Hence,
x\mathrel{{\prec}^{+}}y
.
By Lemma
B.4
,

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
must hold.

∎

The following lemma states that events ordered in real-time
have all their spans disjoint from each other, i.e., spans
do not go beyond the events they belong to.

Lemma B.6
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
If
{x}\sqsubset{y}
then
\forall b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x),b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(y).\ {b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.

Proof.

Let
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x),b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(y)
. By Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::blocks-contained-in-abstract-time-interval}}
,
we have
{y}.\textit{start}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{f({b_{y}})}.\textit{start}
and
there is
i
such that
{l({b_{x}})}\leq^{R}{i}
and

{i}.\textit{end}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{x}.\textit{end}
.

Hence, by definition of
\sqsubset
, we have,

{i}.\textit{end}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{x}.\textit{end}<_{\mathbb{N}}{y}.\textit{start}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{f({b_{y}})}.\textit{start}

This means that
i
finishes in real-time before

f({b_{y}})
starts. But since
\leq^{R}
respects the real time order of rep events
(i.e.,
\leq^{R}
is a linearization),
we have
{i}<^{R}{f({b_{y}})}
.

But
{l({b_{x}})}\leq^{R}{i}
, which means

{l({b_{x}})}<^{R}{f({b_{y}})}
. Therefore,

{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.
∎

We can now prove each visibility axiom.

Lemma B.7
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::non-helpers}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{y}
and
z\in\mathcal{W}_{p}\cap\overline{T}
.

By definition of
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{y}

there are

b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(x)
and
b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(y)
such that

b_{x}
is the maximum satisfying

b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
and

{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.

From hypothesis
z\in\overline{T}
and Lemma
B.5
,

\mathcal{S}(z)\neq\emptyset
holds. So, by
Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::writers-have-writer-blocks}}
,
there is
b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}(z)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.

But
b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
and
b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{p}
,
which means that either
{b_{y}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}
or

{b_{z}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
or
b_{y}=b_{z}
by Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
.

•

Case
{b_{y}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}
. By definition,

{y}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{z}
holds.

•

Case
{b_{z}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.
Again, since
b_{x},b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
, we have three subcases by Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
.

–

Case
{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}
.
We have

b_{x}\sqsubset^{S}b_{z}\sqsubset^{S}b_{y}
, which
is a contradiction, because
b_{x}
was the maximum satisfying
the conditions
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
and

{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
, but now
b_{z}
is a more recent
span satisfying the conditions.

–

Case
{b_{z}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}
. By definition,

{z}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{x}
holds.

–

Case
b_{x}=b_{z}
. We have
\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}(z)\neq\emptyset
, which means
x=z
by Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::runs-are-injective}}
.

•

Case
b_{y}=b_{z}
. We have
\mathcal{S}(y)\cap\mathcal{S}(z)\neq\emptyset
, which means
y=z
by Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::runs-are-injective}}
.

∎

Lemma B.8
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::helped-are-writers}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
x,y\in\overline{T}
, and

{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{y}
.

By definition of
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
,
there is
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(x)
such that
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.
So,
x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
by Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::writers-have-writer-blocks}}
.
∎

Lemma B.9
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::vis-acyclic}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
{x}\prec^{+}{y}
.
For a contradiction, suppose
{y}\sqsubseteq{x}
.

From
{x}\prec^{+}{y}
and Lemma

B.4
,
there are
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x)
and

b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(y)
such that

{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.

From
{y}\sqsubseteq{x}
, we have two cases,

•

Case
{y}\sqsubset{x}
. By Lemma

B.6
,
we also have
{b_{y}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}
.
Hence,
b_{y}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}b_{y}

(Contradiction, since
\sqsubset^{S}
is irreflexive by Lemma
B.1
).

•

Case
y=x
. Hence,
b_{x},b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(x)
.

By Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we also have
f({b_{y}})\leq^{R}l({b_{x}})
.
This means,
l({b_{x}})<^{R}f({b_{y}})\leq^{R}l({b_{x}})
, since
{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.
Hence,
l({b_{x}})<^{R}l({b_{x}})

(Contradiction).

∎

Lemma B.10
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::fin-predicate}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
x\in\overline{T}
. By Lemma
B.5
,

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
holds. Hence, by Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::postcondition-predicate-holds}}
,

o({x})\neq\bot
and
\mathcal{Q}_{{x},{o({x})}}
hold.
In addition, if
x\in T
,
then Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::finished-operations-have-a-run}}
implies

o({x})={x}.\textit{out}
.
∎

Lemma B.11
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::allocs}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
x,y\in\mathcal{A}_{p}\cap\overline{T}
.
By Lemma
B.5
,

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}(y)\neq\emptyset
hold.

By Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::allocs-have-alloc-blocks}}
,
there are
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}\cap\mathcal{S}(x)
and

b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}\cap\mathcal{S}(y)
.
But then
b_{x}=b_{y}
by Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::containment-and-uniqueness-of-alloc-blocks}}
,
which implies
x=y
by Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::runs-are-injective}}
.
∎

Lemma B.12
.

Suppose hypotheses
B.4
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::all-writers-are-willing-helpers}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}\cap\overline{T}
.

By Lemma
B.5
,

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
holds. Hence, by Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::writers-have-writer-blocks}}
,
there is
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.
But then by Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::every-block-must-have-an-allocated-pointer}}

there is
b\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}

such that
{b}\sqsubseteq^{S}{b_{x}}
.

•

Case
b=b_{x}
. By Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::allocs-have-alloc-blocks}}
we have

x\in\mathcal{A}_{p}
, and trivially

{x}\mathrel{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}}_{{p}}{x}
.

•

Case
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}
.
By Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::writer-blocks-belong-to-runs}}
,

b\in\mathcal{S}(z)
for some
z
.
By Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::allocs-have-alloc-blocks}}
we have

z\in\mathcal{A}_{p}
.
But since
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}
, we have

{z}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{x}
by definition.

∎

Theorem B.1
.

If hypotheses
B.4
hold, then

V^{\mu}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})

is valid.

Proof.

All visibility axioms hold by Lemmas from
B.7
to
B.12
.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Span Axioms for RDCSS and helping MCAS

This section focuses on proving that

V^{\mu}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})
is implemented by spans
(for
\mu\in\{\textsc{R},\textsc{M}\}
).
As such, we will define a span structure for RDCSS and MCAS and then
show that this structure implements
V^{\mu}
.
Here,

V^{\textsc{R}}
was defined in

A.6
and

V^{\textsc{M}}
in
A.12
.
Also, we use relations
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
and

\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}

as defined in
B.3

This section uses the notion of span structure (Definition
B.1
)
and implementation of span structure (Definition
B.2
).
See also the concepts in Section
5.2
.

C.1
RDCSS

The full pseudocode for RDCSS is shown in
Figure
13
. The exportable
procedures are
rdcss
,
rread
,

rCAS
,
rwrite
,
rread-c
,

rCAS-c
,
rwrite-c
, and

ralloc
.

Val
denotes the set of all
possible input values.
Val
contains
neither RDCSS descriptors nor pointers storing RDCSS descriptors.

RDCSS requires that every pointer exposed to the clients be classified
as a control or data pointer (
cptr
and
dptr
,
respectively). The data pointers may store values in
Val
,
descriptors, and pointers. Control
pointers can only store values in
Val
.

No particular implementation is provided for boolean predicate

\textit{is\_rdesc}(p)
, but it is assumed that it returns
true if and only if
p
is a pointer storing an RDCSS descriptor.
For example,
[
11
]
suggests that
\textit{is\_rdesc}(p)

could be implemented by checking a reserved bit in
p
; this
reserved bit indicates whether or not the pointer stores a descriptor.

The implementation makes the following assumptions:

•

The alloc at line
9

creates a data pointer
d
such that
\textit{is\_rdesc}(d)
returns true.

•

No implementation details are provided for the primitive allocs at
lines
66
and

68
, but they are assumed to
have linearizable implementations.

•

Any input data pointer
p:\textsc{dptr}
to any
exportable procedure must satisfy
\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}(p)
.

•

Any RDCSS descriptor
desc
must satisfy

\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}({desc}.pt_{1})
and

\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}({desc}.pt_{2})
.

•

Any input pointer to any procedure must have been previously
allocated with an invocation to
ralloc
.

•

The allocs at either line
66

or line
68

return a pointer
p
such that
\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}(p)
.

We now define the span structure for RDCSS.

Definition C.1

(Span Structure for RDCSS)
.

We denote the structure by
S^{\textsc{R}}
.

A span is either a 2-tuple of the form
(a,b)
or
a 1-tuple of the form
(a)
, where
a,b
are rep events in the execution history.

For each pointer
p
, set
\mathcal{S}_{p}
is defined by the
following list of spans containing rep events,

1.

Any
(b)
such that,

•

p
is a
dptr
pointer

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-CAS-RDCSS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b
is a failed
CAS
reading pointer
p

2.

Any
(b,c)
such that,

•

p
is a
dptr
pointer

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-CAS-RDCSS}

•

{c}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-pt2-write-success-RDCSS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b
executes before
c
and there is no other
p
-write rep event between
b
and
c
.

•

b
and
c
are successful
CAS
es having the form

b=\textit{CAS}(p,\_,n)
and
c=\textit{CAS}(p,n,\_)
for some
n

such that
\textit{is\_rdesc}(n)

3.

Any
(b,c)
such that,

•

p
is a
dptr
pointer

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-CAS-RDCSS}

•

{c}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-pt2-write-fail-RDCSS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b
executes before
c
and there is no other
p
-write rep event between
b
and
c
.

•

b
and
c
are successful
CAS
es having the form

b=\textit{CAS}(p,e,n)
and
c=\textit{CAS}(p,n,e)
for some

such that
\textit{is\_rdesc}(n)

4.

Any
(b)
such that,

•

p
is a
cptr
pointer

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-pt1-read-RDCSS}

•

b
reads pointer
p

5.

Any
(b)
such that,

•

p
is a
dptr
pointer

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-access-Read-RDCSS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b
reads pointer
p

6.

Any
(b)
such that,

•

p

pointer

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-access-CAS-RDCSS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

p

Any
(b)
such that,

•

p

With this, the set of writer spans
\mathcal{W}_{p}
can be defined as,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\{(b)\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\mid b\text{ writes or allocs pointer }p\}\cup\{(b,c)\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\mid{c}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-pt2-write-success-RDCSS}\}

The set of alloc spans
\mathcal{A}_{p}
as follows,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\{(b)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\mid b\text{ allocs pointer }p\wedge({b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alloc-control-Alloc-RDCSS}\vee{p}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alloc-data-Alloc-RDCSS})\}

The list defining
\mathcal{S}_{p}
could contain further
single-instruction spans (for example, by including
code lines
9
,

23
, and

46
), but the listed
ones define the minimal set that will allow us to prove
the span axioms.

Notice that the only spans in
\mathcal{S}_{p}
with
more than one instruction are those of the form

(b,c)
, where
b
is the instruction that writes the
descriptor and
c
is the instruction that resolves the
descriptor.

Also, notice that the only spans of the form
(b,c)

that are considered to be writer spans in set

\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
are those where
c
successfully
updates the pointer to its new value at Line

26
. In
other words, spans that “undo” the descriptor
by successfully executing the CAS at
Line
28

are not considered to be writer spans, since they
belong to a failed RDCSS.

We argue that
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
is well-defined.
For that matter, we need to show that the
function’s conditions pick spans uniquely.

First, let us focus on the
rdcss
cases. Notice that whenever
an
\textit{rdcss}(desc)
invocation starts, a fresh pointer

d
is created at Line
9

to store the descriptor
desc
.
This pointer
d
will serve as unique identifier for
the invocation, because the invoking thread executes
Line
9
only once.

The invoking thread then enters the
\textit{rdcss}^{\prime}

procedure and tries to write
d
into pointer

{desc}.pt_{2}
by attempting the CAS at Line

13
.

If the CAS fails and its output
v
satisfies

\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}(v)
, then the invoking
thread will finish the invocation by returning
v
.
Notice that once these
two conditions are satisfied (i.e. CAS fails and

\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}(v)
holds), there will
be no more attempts of executing Line

13
.
In other words, there is at most one instruction
of the form

\textit{CAS}({desc}.pt_{2},{desc}.exp_{2},d)

at line code
13
, such that
it is a failing CAS and its output does not
satisfy
is_rdesc
.
Therefore, if there is at least one such instruction,
it will be unique. This justifies case 1.(a) of

\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
(also, by case (1) in the definition of set

\mathcal{S}_{{desc}.pt_{2}}
).

On the contrary, if the instruction

\textit{CAS}({desc}.pt_{2},{desc}.exp_{2},d)

at Line
13
succeeds,
then this CAS with those parameters cannot succeed again: the
thread will invoke the
complete
procedure
afterwards and finish by returning
{desc}.exp_{2}
,
and other threads cannot execute Line

13
with parameter
d

because those threads must have executed Line

9
to
create a fresh pointer to pass to the CAS
(i.e. their CAS will have the form

\textit{CAS}(\_,\_,d^{\prime})
, where
d^{\prime}\neq d
).

Therefore, since

\textit{CAS}({desc}.pt_{2},{desc}.exp_{2},d)

at Line
13
can succeed
at most once, either of

\textit{CAS}({desc}.pt_{2},d,{desc}.new_{2})
,

\textit{CAS}({desc}.pt_{2},d,{desc}.exp_{2})

at Lines
26

and
28
can
succeed at most once because
these CASes expect the value
d
to be present
in the pointer and
d
can only be written by the
CAS at Line
13
.

This justifies cases 1.(b) and 1.(c) of

\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
. Notice that the read
r

in those cases is uniquely chosen because it is
required to be the
last
read
before
c_{2}
such that
r
was executed by the thread that
successfully executed
c_{2}
.

For the cases
x=\textit{rread}(p)
and

x=\textit{rCAS}(p,e,n)
,
their condition is choosing instructions at Lines

31
and

38

such that their output do not satisfy

is_rdesc
(by definition of set
\mathcal{S}_{p}
).
But this condition can happen at most once during the
invocation of
x
,
because the invoking thread finishes
x
once
the condition is satisfied.

The cases for
x=\textit{rwrite}(p,v)
follow a
similar strategy. While the cases for

x=\textit{ralloc}(v,k)
are trivial, since the
allocs execute at most once within the invocation of

x
.

Finally, the cases for
x=\textit{rread-c}
,
rCAS-c
,
and
rwrite-c
are trivial, since
their code execute at most once during their invocation.

We are ready to prove that

V^{\textsc{R}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})
is implemented by span
structure
S^{\textsc{R}}
, i.e., we are going to prove that
the span axioms of Figure
12
are satisfied. We require a couple of lemmas.

Lemma C.1
.

If
i_{1}
,
i_{2}
are allocs at either
Line
66
or Line

68

such that they allocate the same pointer, then
i_{1}=i_{2}
.

Proof.

Allocs create fresh pointers, and pointers are not deallocated.
Therefore, if
{i_{1}}<^{R}{i_{2}}
, then
i_{2}
must create
a pointer that is different from the one created by
i_{1}
(Contradicting
the hypothesis).
Similarly, if
{i_{2}}<^{R}{i_{1}}
.
Hence,
i_{1}=i_{2}
.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

If
b\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
,
then there is
x
such that
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)
and
\textsc{writer}\ {x}\ {p}\ {v}
,
where
v
is the value written by
l({b})
.

Proof.

Each span in
\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
occurs in one of the cases in
the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
.

For example, consider the span of the form
(b,c)
at entry 2 in the definition of
\mathcal{S}_{p}
.
Since
(b,c)
contains the successful CASes at lines
13

and
26
, those CASes must have the
form
\textit{CAS}({desc}.pt_{2},{desc}.exp_{2},d)
and

\textit{CAS}({desc}.pt_{2},d,{desc}.new_{2})
, for some
desc
,
d
, because
the CASes are instances of the code. In addition,
d
must have been generated
by executing line
9
by some invocation
of procedure
\textit{rdcss}(desc)
. Even more, the thread
T
that executed line

26
must have executed the read
at line
24
, and this read must have occurred
after
d
was written at Line
13
, otherwise,
T

would not be helping
d
at the
complete
procedure.

All the above satisfy case 1.(b) in the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
.
Notice that
l({c})
writes value
{desc}.new_{2}
at
{desc}.pt_{2}
, and

\textsc{writer}\ {x}\ {{desc}.pt_{2}}\ {{desc}.new_{2}}
holds by
definition of
writer
for the
rdcss
case.

The rest of spans in
\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
are even simpler, because
they are 1-tuple spans of the form
(b)
, and
b
is executed by the same thread
that invoked the exportable procedure that
b
is part of.
∎

Lemma C.3
.

Let
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
. If

\textsc{writer}\ {x}\ {p}\ {v}
, then
l({b})
writes value

v
into
p
.

Proof.

By simple inspection on the cases for

\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
and
writer
.
∎

Lemma C.4
.

Suppose
x
is one of
rdcss
,
rCAS
,
rread
,
rCAS-c
,
or
rread-c
. Suppose
p\in\textsc{pts}
and
v\in\textsc{Val}
. Then,
the following statements are equivalent,

•

There is
y
such that
{y}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {y}\ {p}\ {v}
.

•

There is
b\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(x)
such that
f({b})
reads value
v
.

Proof.

We prove each direction.

\Longrightarrow
. By definition of
{y}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{x}
,
there are
b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(y)
,

b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(x)
such that
b_{y}
is the most recent span under
\sqsubseteq^{S}

satisfying
b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
and
{b_{y}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}
.
By Lemma
C.3
,

l({b_{y}})
writes value
v
into
p
.

We argue that any
p
-mutating rep event in between
l({b_{y}})
and
f({b_{x}})
must eventually
restore the value to
v
before
f({b_{x}})
reads
p
.

If the
p
-write rep events correspond to lines
38
,

51
,

59
,

62
, they would
belong to a 1-span, contradicting that there are no
p
-writing spans in between

b_{y}
and
b_{x}
. So, let us focus on rep events at lines
13
,

26
, and
28
.
If the CAS at line
13
succeeds, no other
p
-write rep event can occur
up to the point where the descriptor is resolved, and any read would discover the descriptor up
to the point where the descriptor is resolved. Therefore
f({b_{x}})

must occur after the descriptor is resolved by either line

26
or
28
.
If resolution happens at line
26
, we would have
a
p
-write span in between
b_{y}
and
b_{x}
which is impossible by hypothesis. Therefore,
resolution must happen at line
28
.
But line
28
undoes or restores to whatever value was in
p

before line
13
wrote the descriptor.
This means that if
v
was in
p
before line
13
executed,

v
will be in
p
after line
28
executes.
Therefore
v
is preserved and
f({b_{x}})
reads
v
.

\Longleftarrow
. Let
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(x)

such that
f({b_{x}})
reads value
v\in\textsc{Val}
.
Therefore, there is a most recent rep event
w
that wrote
v
into
p
.
If
w
corresponds to line
28

then
w
is the last rep event in a span that failed
to modify the pointer, which means that the pointer is getting restored
to the value it had before the descriptor was written at line
13
.
If this is the case, we just keep searching for a
p
-write rep event of
v

that does not correspond to line
28
.
This rep event exists because there is an alloc of
p
as first rep event for
p
.
If
w^{\prime}
is the chosen
p
-write event, we will have
w^{\prime}=l({b})
for some span
b
in
\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
,
since the set
\mathcal{S}_{p}
covers all possible places where a write of a
Val

value could occur in the code. Span
b
will be the most recent
p
-write span
before
b_{x}
, since all the more recent spans we found correspond to failing spans resolving
at line
28
.
By Lemma
C.2
,
there is
y
such that
b\in\mathcal{S}(y)
and
\textsc{writer}\ {y}\ {p}\ {v}
.
Therefore,
{y}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{x}

by definition.
∎

Lemma C.5
.

Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
holds.

Proof.

Since this is trivial when
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
are 1-tuple spans,
we only need to consider the cases
when
b_{1}
is a 1-span and
b_{2}
a 2-tuple span or when both

b_{1}
and
b_{2}
are 2-tuple spans.

•

Case
b_{1}=(c)
is a 1-tuple span and
b_{2}=(e,g)
a 2-tuple span.

Since
b_{2}
is a 2-tuple, by definition of
\mathcal{S}_{p}
, pointer
p
is of type

dptr
. Since every procedure invocation requires that input pointers be previously
allocated, there must be some
dptr
rep alloc
i
such that

{i}<^{R}{e}
. We also know that there cannot be a
p
-writer rep in
between
e
and
g
, because once a descriptor is written by
e
, no other write can
occur until the descriptor is removed by
g
.

If
c
is an alloc, by Lemma
C.1
,

c=i
and
{b_{1}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{2}}
follows. So, we can assume that
c
is not an alloc.

By going through all the
dptr
pointer cases for
(c)\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
,
we see that all the cases imply
\neg\textit{is\_rdesc}({c}.\textit{out})
,
i.e.,
c
does not read a descriptor.

Therefore,
c
cannot occur in between
e
and
g
, because
e
writes a descriptor.
Also, the cases
c=e
or
c=g
are excluded because
\mathcal{S}_{p}

is defined so that 1-spans and 2-tuple spans do not share rep events.

•

Case
b_{1}=(c,d)
is a 2-tuple span and
b_{2}=(e,g)
a 2-tuple span.

By definition of
\mathcal{S}_{p}
, we know there is no writer into
p

in between
c
and
d
and in between
e
and
g
. But
c
,
d
,
e
,
g

are writers into
p
by definition of
\mathcal{S}_{p}
.
Therefore, we must have
{e}<^{R}{c}
, or

e=c
, or
{d}<^{R}{e}
(the case
d=e
is impossible, because

d
and
e
correspond to different code lines:
e
writes a descriptor, while

d
removes it).

–

Case
{d}<^{R}{e}
. Hence
{b_{1}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{2}}
.

–

Case
{e}<^{R}{c}
. We have either
{g}<^{R}{c}
, or

g=d
, or
{d}<^{R}{g}
(the case
g=c
is impossible,
because
g
removes a descriptor, while
c
writes one).

The cases
g=d
and
{d}<^{R}{g}
would lead to

e<^{R}c<^{R}g
(Contradiction).
Therefore,
{g}<^{R}{c}
and
{b_{2}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{1}}
follows.

–

Case
e=c
. We have either

g=d
or
{d}<^{R}{g}
(the case
{g}<^{R}{c}
is
impossible because
g
occurs after
e=c
).

But the case
{d}<^{R}{g}
leads to

e<^{R}d<^{R}g
(Contradiction).
Therefore
g=d
also holds and
b_{1}=b_{2}
follows.

∎

Lemma C.6
.

Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
holds.

Proof.

This is trivial when
x
has a denotation with only one span.
So, it is enough to check the two cases for
x=\textit{rdcss}(desc)
when
the denotation is of the form
\mathcal{S}(x)=\{(c_{1},c_{2}),\ (r)\}
.

But in both cases we have
c_{1}\leq^{R}r\leq^{R}c_{2}

because the control read
r
and descriptor removal
c_{2}
are executed by the same thread
inside the
complete
procedure, in that order.
Additionally,
r
must occur after
c_{1}
because
r
occurs inside the

complete
procedure, which can only be invoked after the descriptor written
by
c_{1}
is discovered.
Therefore, it does not matter how we choose

b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathcal{S}(x)
, we will always have

{f({b_{1}})}\leq^{R}{l({b_{2}})}
.
∎

Lemma C.7
.

Axioms

S_{\ref{principle::finished-operations-have-a-run}}
holds.

Proof.

We prove each procedure in turn.

•

Case
\textit{rdcss}(desc)
. Suppose that
\textit{rdcss}(desc)
finished.
Then, the invoking thread
T
must have created a pointer
d
storing
desc

at line
9
, and finished the invocation of
\textit{rdcss}^{\prime}(d,desc)
.
Since
\textit{rdcss}^{\prime}
is a recursive method and we know that
T

finished executing it, this means that
T
must have reached an
invocation of
\textit{rdcss}^{\prime}
that did not recurse, i.e., an invocation
where lines
15
-
16
were not executed. So, we can assume we are on
such execution. Since
T
reached line 20, it means that the CAS at
line
13
must have returned a non-descriptor value
v
(otherwise,
T

would have enter the conditional at line
14
, executing lines
15
-
16
).

–

Case
v\neq{desc}.exp_{2}
. Then the CAS at line
13
failed,
T
did not
enter the conditional at line
14
and returned
v
. This corresponds to
(1).(a) in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
with output whatever line
13

returned (which is equal to
v
) and the denotation is not empty.

–

Case
v={desc}.exp_{2}
. Then the CAS at line
13
wrote the descriptor

d
, and
T
terminated
\textit{complete}(d)
at line 19 before returning

at

T
terminated
\textit{complete}(d)
,

must have reached
either line

or
28
.

*

Subcase:
T
reached line 26. Let us suppose that the CAS at line

T
(so that the descriptor was removed and
replaced with the new value). The read at line 24 must have
returned
{desc}.exp_{1}
. Since
T
executed both lines 24 and 26, this
corresponds to (1).(b) in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
, with output

{desc}.exp_{2}=v
, and the denotation is not empty. Let us now suppose that the
CAS at line 26 did not succeed for
T
. This means that some other
thread
T^{\prime}
has already removed the descriptor, which means that

T^{\prime}
successfully executed either line 26 or 28. If
T^{\prime}
reached
line 26, then
T^{\prime}
must have read
{desc}.exp_{1}
at line 24 and once
again this corresponds to (1).(b) in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
, with
output
{desc}.exp_{2}=v
and the denotation is not empty. If
T^{\prime}
reached line 28,
then
T^{\prime}
must have read a value different from
{desc}.exp_{1}
at line
24, in which case, (1).(c) applies in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
, with
output
{desc}.exp_{2}=v
and the denotation is not empty.

*

Subcase:
T
reached line 28. Let us suppose that the CAS at line
28 succeeded for
T
(so that the descriptor was removed and the
pointer restored to the expected value). The read at line 24
must have returned a value different from
{desc}.exp_{1}
. Since
T

executed both lines 24 and 28, this corresponds to (1).(c) in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
,
with output
{desc}.exp_{2}=v
, and the denotation is not
empty. Let us now suppose that the CAS at line 28 did not
succeed for
T
. This means that some other thread
T^{\prime}
has already
removed the descriptor, which means that
T^{\prime}
successfully
executed either line 26 or 28. If
T^{\prime}
reached line 26, then
T^{\prime}

must have read
{desc}.exp_{1}
at line 24 and this corresponds to
(1).(b) in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
, with output
{desc}.exp_{2}=v

and the denotation is
not empty. If
T^{\prime}
reached line 28, then
T^{\prime}
must have read a value
different from
{desc}.exp_{1}
at line 24, in which case, (1).(c)
applies in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
, with output
{desc}.exp_{2}=v
and
the denotation is
not empty.

•

Case
\textit{rread}(p)
. Suppose that
\textit{rread}(p)

Let

T

Lemma C.8
.

Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
holds.

Proof.

Trivial for 1-spans. For 2-tuple spans
(a,b)
, the code can only remove descriptors (rep event
b
)
only if it was previously written at line
13
(rep event
a
).
∎

Lemma C.9
.

Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::runs-are-injective}}
holds.

Proof.

First, let us focus when neither
x
nor
y
are
rdcss
events.

If
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}(y)
, then (by going through all the
non-
rdcss
cases in
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
),

b=(i_{1})
for some
i_{1}
that is executed by the thread that invoked
x
and

b=(i_{2})
for some
i_{2}
that is executed by the thread that invoked
y
.
In addition,
i_{1}
occurs within the invocation of
x
and
i_{2}
within
the invocation of
y
. Therefore,
i_{1}=i_{2}
, which means that
x
and
y

are invoked by the same thread.

If
x\neq y
, then
x
and
y
cannot overlap in real-time, because they
are invoked by the same thread. But this contradicts that
i_{1}
occurs within
the invocation of both
x
and
y
. Therefore,
x=y
.

Now, let us check the case when either
x
is an
rdcss
event or
y
is.
Say,
x
is an
rdcss
event.

It must be the case that either
f({b})
has code line

13
or
24
.

If
f({b})
has code line
13
, then
it has the form
\textit{CAS}(\_,\_,d)
where
d
is the unique
identifier for the invocation
x
generated at line

9
(see discussion that the denotation
is well-defined). Since
b
also occurs in the denotation of
y
and
d
is unique
per-invocation, it must be the case that
x=y
.

If
f({b})
has code line
24
,
then by definition of
\mathcal{S}(x)
,
f({b})
is the last
read carried out by some thread
T
before it successfully executed

\textit{CAS}(\_,d,\_)

(line
26
or
line
28
),
where
d
is the unique id of invocation
x
.
Since
b
also occurs in the denotation of
y
, event
y
must
also be an
rdcss
, and
f({b})
is the last
read carried out by the same thread
T
(it must be the same thread because no two threads
can execute the
same
rep event
f({b})
) before it successfully executed

\textit{CAS}(\_,d^{\prime},\_)
,
where
d^{\prime}
is the unique id of invocation
y
.

Therefore,
\textit{CAS}(\_,d,\_)
and
\textit{CAS}(\_,d^{\prime},\_)

must be the same rep event (since they are both the most immediate CAS executed
by
T
after
f({b})
).
This means that
d=d^{\prime}
, which implies
x=y
since ids are unique per invocation.
∎

Lemma C.10
.

Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::writer-blocks-belong-to-runs}}
holds.

Proof.

Directly from Lemma
C.2
.
∎

Lemma C.11
.

Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::postcondition-predicate-holds}}
holds.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
. We see from definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
that all non-empty cases have
o({x})\neq\bot
.
To prove
\mathcal{Q}_{{x},{o({x})}}
, we do a case analysis on
x
.

•

Case
x=\textit{rdcss}(d)
. We want to prove,

\displaystyle\exists z_{2}.\ {z_{2}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{d}.pt_{2}}}{x}\wedge\textsc{writer}\ {z_{2}}\ {{d}.pt_{2}}\ {o({x})}\wedge{}

\displaystyle(o({x})={d}.exp_{2}\rightarrow\exists z_{1}.\ {z_{1}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{d}.pt_{1}}}{x})

All the three cases in the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket

have a span
b
such that
f({b})
is the CAS at line
13
.
In all the three cases, the value read by this CAS serves as denotation output, even when
the CAS succeeds, in which case the CAS read the value
{d}.exp_{2}
.
Therefore, by Lemma
C.4
,
there is
z_{2}
such that
{z_{2}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{d}.pt_{2}}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {z_{2}}\ {{d}.pt_{2}}\ {o({x})}
.

Suppose
o({x})={d}.exp_{2}
. Hence, the CAS at line
13

must have succeeded, and the only two applicable cases in the definition of

\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
have a span
r
such that
f({r})
reads some value

v^{\prime}
in pointer
{d}.pt_{1}
at Line
24
.
Therefore, by Lemma
C.4
,
there is
z_{1}
such that
{z_{1}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{d}.pt_{1}}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {z_{2}}\ {{d}.pt_{1}}\ {v^{\prime}}
.

•

Case
x=\textit{ralloc}(v,k)
. We want to prove
o({x})={x}.\textit{out}

and
{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
. But this follows trivially from the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
.

•

Case
x=\textit{rCAS}(q,e,n)
. We want to prove,

\displaystyle\exists z.\ {z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}{x}\wedge\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {q}\ {o({x})}

The only case in the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket

has a span
b
such that
f({b})
reads value
o({x})

in pointer
q
at line
38
.
Therefore, by Lemma
C.4
,
there is
z
such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {q}\ {o({x})}
.

•

Case
x=\textit{rwrite}(q,v)
. We want to prove
o({x})=tt

but this follows trivially from the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
.

•

Case
x=\textit{rread}(q)
. We want to prove,

\displaystyle\exists z.\ {z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}{x}\wedge\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {q}\ {o({x})}

The only case in the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket

has a span
b
such that
f({b})
reads value
o({x})

in pointer
q
at line
31
.
Therefore, by Lemma
C.4
,
there is
z
such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {q}\ {o({x})}
.

•

The cases for
rCAS-c
,
rwrite-c
, and
rread-c
are similar
to the cases for
rCAS
,
rwrite
, and
rread
, respectively.

∎

Lemma C.12
.

Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::writers-have-writer-blocks}}
holds.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
. We do a case analysis on
x
.

•

Case
x=\textit{rdcss}(d)
.

\Longrightarrow
. By definition of
\mathcal{W}_{p}
, we have
p={d}.pt_{2}
and,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\exists z_{1},z_{2}.\ {z_{1}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{d}.pt_{1}}}{x}\wedge{z_{2}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{d}.pt_{2}}}{x}\wedge{}\\
\textsc{writer}\ {z_{1}}\ {{d}.pt_{1}}\ {{d}.exp_{1}}\wedge\textsc{writer}\ {z_{2}}\ {{d}.pt_{2}}\ {{d}.exp_{2}}\end{split}

(22)

By Lemma
C.4
, there are

b_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{{d}.pt_{1}}(x)
,

b_{2}\in\mathcal{S}_{{d}.pt_{2}}(x)
such that

f({b_{1}})
reads value
{d}.exp_{1}
and

f({b_{2}})
reads value
{d}.exp_{2}
.

By definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
,
b_{2}
must be a 2-tuple span where
f({b_{2}})
is the successful CAS
at line
13
and
b_{1}
must be a 1-span where
f({b_{1}})
is
the read at line
24
. Since

f({b_{1}})
reads the expected value, the thread must have executed the successful CAS at line

26
at rep event
l({b_{2}})
.

Therefore,
b_{2}\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{d}.pt_{2}}
.

\Longleftarrow
. Suppose
b_{2}\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.
By the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
,
b_{2}
must be one of the two kinds of 2-tuple spans.
In both cases of the 2-tuple spans,

f({b_{2}})
reads value
{d}.exp_{2}
since the
CAS at line
13
succeeded.
But by definition of
\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
,
l({b_{2}})
must
be the successful CAS at line
26
.
Then,
p={d}.pt_{2}

since
l({b_{2}})
writes into
p
(by definition of
\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
)
and into
{d}.pt_{2}
(by definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
).

Also, by definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
, there is
f({b_{1}})
in the denotation that reads value
{d}.exp_{1}

at pointer
{d}.pt_{1}

because the CAS at line
26
succeeded,
which means that the thread must have read the expected value at line
24
.

Hence, (
22
)
follows by Lemma
C.4
.

•

Case
x=\textit{ralloc}(v,k)
.

\Longrightarrow
. Since
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
, by definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
,

{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
,
{x}.\textit{out}\neq\bot
, and there is
a span in
\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{x}.\textit{out}}
.
But since
x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
, we have
p={x}.\textit{out}
.

\Longleftarrow
. Let
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.
By definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
,
{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
,
{x}.\textit{out}\neq\bot
,

b=(i)
for some alloc
i
at either line

66
or line
68
, and
(i)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{x}.\textit{out}}
.

But from
(i)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
we also know that
i
allocates
p
,
and since allocations are unique,
p={x}.\textit{out}
. Hence,
x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
.

•

Case
x=\textit{rCAS}(q,e,n)
.

\Longrightarrow
. By definition of
\mathcal{W}_{p}
, we have
p=q
and,

\displaystyle\exists z.\ {z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{x}\wedge\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {p}\ {e}

(23)

By Lemma
C.4
, there is

b\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(x)
such that

f({b})
reads value
e
.

By definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
,
f({b})
must be the successful CAS
at line
38
.

Therefore,
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.

\Longleftarrow
. Suppose
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
. Then,
p=q

by definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
, since the same rep event writes into

p
(by definition of
\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
) and into
q
(by definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
).
Also,
f({b})
reads value
e
since the
CAS at line
38
must succeed because
b\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.

Hence, (
23
)
follows by Lemma
C.4
.

•

Case
x=\textit{rwrite}(q,v)
.

\Longrightarrow
. Since
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
, there is
a span in
\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{q}
because all
spans in the denotation write at
q
. But
p=q
follows from

x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
.

\Longleftarrow
. Suppose
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
. Then,
p=q

since the same rep event writes into
p
(by definition of
\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
)
and into
q
(by definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
), which means
x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
.

•

Case
x=\textit{rread}(q)
. Trivial since
x
is neither a successful writer
nor it has writer spans in its denotation.

•

The cases for
rCAS-c
,
rwrite-c
, and
rread-c
are similar
to the cases for
rCAS
,
rwrite
, and
rread
, respectively.

∎

Lemma C.13
.

Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::blocks-contained-in-abstract-time-interval}}
holds.

Proof.

We prove each item.

•

(i).
Let us focus on all denotations in
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
having
the form
\{(b)\}
.
In these denotations,
b
is a rep event
that is invoked by the same thread that invoked
x
. Hence,
b
starts after
x

started.

Now, for the cases producing denotations of the form
\{(c_{1},c_{2}),\ (r)\}
.
c_{1}
can only
execute after
x
was started because line
13
executes
after line
9
is executed by the thread invoking

x
. Also,
r
can only execute after
x
started because threads enter
the
complete
procedure only after the descriptor identifier has been written
at Line
13
by
c_{1}
.

•

(ii).
Let us focus on all denotations in
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
having
the form
\{(b)\}
.
In these denotations,
b
is a rep event
that is invoked by the same thread that invoked
x
. Hence,
b
finishes before
x

finishes (hence, in this cases, choose
i\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}b
).

Now, for the cases producing denotations of the form
\{(c_{1},c_{2}),\ (r)\}
. Let
T
be the thread
that invoked
x
and
c_{1}
. At
c_{1}
,
T
wrote the descriptor identifier at Line

13
and now it is about to enter the
complete

procedure.

If the thread that executes
c_{2}
and
r
is also
T
, then choose
i\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}c_{2}
for any
of the spans
(c_{1},c_{2})
and
(r)
, since
r
must have finished before
c_{2}
started.

If the thread that executes
c_{2}
and
r
is another thread
T^{\prime}
, then
T^{\prime}

successfully executed one of the CASes at Lines

26
and
28

together with the read at line
24
.
Therefore, once
T
enters the
complete
procedure,
T
will attempt one of the CASes and fail.
Hence,
T
will discover that the descriptor has been resolved already, and then it will finish
x
.
In this case, for any of the spans, choose
i
to be the failed CAS executed by
T
.
Notice that we cannot choose

i
to be
c_{2}
, because
c_{2}
might not have finished by the time
T
finished the invocation of
x

(i.e.
T^{\prime}
carried out the effect of
c_{2}
but
T^{\prime}
might not have finished
c_{2}
yet).

∎

Lemma C.14
.

Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::containment-and-uniqueness-of-alloc-blocks}}
holds.

Proof.

If
b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
,
then by definition of
\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
,

b_{1}
and
b_{2}
are 1-tuples allocating
p
by executing either
Line
66
or line

68
.
But by Lemma

C.1
,

b_{1}=b_{2}
.
∎

Lemma C.15
.

Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::every-block-must-have-an-allocated-pointer}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
b\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
.
Each procedure requires that its input pointers be created by a previous alloc invocation.
Therefore, there is some rep alloc
i
that allocates
p
and executes either
line
66
or line

68
. So that

i
executes before
f({b})
(in case
b
does not contain an alloc), or
b=(i)
.

Now, any rep event which is an instance of lines
66
and line

68
belongs to some span by definition of
\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
.
Therefore,
(i)\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
.

So, if
i
executes before
f({b})
, then
{(i)}\sqsubset^{S}{b}
, and if

b=(i)
, then
{(i)}\sqsubseteq^{S}{b}
.
∎

Lemma C.16
.

Axioms

S_{\ref{principle::allocs-have-alloc-blocks}}
holds.

Proof.

We prove each direction.

\Longrightarrow
. Since
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
and
x\in\mathcal{A}_{p}
then
x
is an alloc and

{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
,
{x}.\textit{out}\neq\bot
, and
p={x}.\textit{out}
. But by definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
,
there is
(i)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{x}.\textit{out}}
, where
i
is either line

66
or line
68
.
Therefore,
(i)\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
by definition.

\Longleftarrow
. Let
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
.
By definition of
\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
,
f({b})
allocates
p
and it is either
line
66
or line
68
.
But the only case applicable in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
is when
x
is an alloc, which means

{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
,
{x}.\textit{out}\neq\bot
and
b\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{x}.\textit{out}}
.
Also, since rep allocs are unique
p={x}.\textit{out}
, which
means
x\in\mathcal{A}_{p}
.
∎

Theorem C.1
.

V^{\textsc{R}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})

is implemented by
span structure
S^{\textsc{R}}
.

Proof.

All span axioms hold from Lemma
C.5
to
Lemma
C.16
.
∎

Theorem C.2
.

The RDCSS implementation of Figure
13
is linearizable.

Proof.

By Theorem
A.1
, it
suffices to show that

V^{\textsc{R}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})
is valid. But by
Theorem
B.1
, it suffices that

V^{\textsc{R}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})
is implemented by span
structure
S^{\textsc{R}}
. This is given by Theorem

C.1
.
∎

C.2
MCAS

The full pseudocode for MCAS is shown in
Figure
14
. MCAS uses the exportable
procedures of RDCSS as primitives.
The exportable procedures for MCAS are
mcas
,
mread
,

mwrite
, and
malloc
.

Val
denotes the set of all
possible input values.
Val
contains
neither MCAS descriptors nor pointers storing MCAS descriptors.

No particular implementation is provided for boolean predicate

\textit{is\_mdesc}(p)
, but it is assumed that it returns
true if and only if
p
is a pointer storing an MCAS descriptor.
For example,
[
11
]
suggests that
\textit{is\_mdesc}(p)

could be implemented by checking a reserved bit in
p
; this
reserved bit indicates whether or not the pointer stores a descriptor.

The implementation makes the following assumptions:

•

The list of entries given as input to procedure
mcas

is not empty.

•

The alloc at line
32

creates a pointer
d
such that
\textit{is\_mdesc}(d)
returns true.

•

Any input pointer
p:\textsc{pts}
to any
exportable procedure must satisfy
\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}(p)
.

•

Any input pointer to any procedure must have been previously
allocated with an invocation to
malloc
.

•

The alloc at line
28

returns a pointer
p
such that
\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}(p)
.

1:

update_entry
:

pt
:
dptr

,

:

status
:

,

,

Figure 14
:
MCAS implementation. It uses as primitives the exportable procedures in RDCSS.

We now define the span structure for MCAS.

Definition C.2

(Span Structure for Helping MCAS)
.

We denote the structure by
S^{\textsc{M}}
.

A span is either a 2-tuple of the form
(a,b)
or
a 1-tuple of the form
(a)
, where
a,b
are rep events in the execution history.

For each pointer
p
, set
\mathcal{S}_{p}
is defined by the
following list of spans containing rep events,

1.

Any
(b)
such that,

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::invoke-rdcss-in-MCAS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b=\textit{rdcss}(desc)
for some
exp
and
desc=\textsc{rdesc}(\_,p,\textsc{Undec},exp,\_)

such that
{b}.\textit{out}\neq exp

2.

Any
(b,c)
such that,

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::invoke-rdcss-in-MCAS}

•

{c}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::resolve-status-MCAS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b
executes before
c
and there is no other
p
-write rep event between
b
and
c
.

•

b=\textit{rdcss}(desc)
and
c=\textit{rCAS}(s,\textsc{Undec},\textsc{Succ})
,
for some
s
,
exp
,
d
, and
desc=\textsc{rdesc}(s,p,\textsc{Undec},exp,d)
such that

c
is successful,
{b}.\textit{out}=exp
, and
\textit{is\_mdesc}(d)
.

3.

Any
(b,c)
such that,

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::invoke-rdcss-in-MCAS}

•

{c}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::resolve-status-MCAS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b
executes before
c
and there is no other
p
-write rep event between
b
and
c
.

•

b=\textit{rdcss}(desc)
and
c=\textit{rCAS}(s,\textsc{Undec},\textsc{Fail})
,
for some
s
,
exp
,
d
, and
desc=\textsc{rdesc}(s,p,\textsc{Undec},exp,d)
such that

c
is successful,
{b}.\textit{out}=exp
, and
\textit{is\_mdesc}(d)
.

4.

Any
(b)
such that,

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-access-Read-MCAS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b
reads pointer
p

5.

Any
(b)
such that,

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alg-attempt-write-Write-MCAS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b
is a successful
rCAS
writing into pointer
p

6.

Any
(b)
such that
{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alloc-data-Alloc-MCAS}
and

b
has as output pointer
p
.

With this, the set of writer spans
\mathcal{W}_{p}
can be defined as,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\{(b)\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\mid b\text{ writes or allocs pointer }p\}\cup\{(b,c)\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\mid c=\textit{rCAS}(\_,\textsc{Undec},\textsc{Succ})\}

The set of alloc spans
\mathcal{A}_{p}
as follows,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\{(b)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\mid b\text{ allocs pointer }p\wedge{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alloc-data-Alloc-MCAS}\}

We now define the denotation
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
by cases on
event
x
,

1.

Case
x=\textit{mcas}(\overline{u})
.

(a)

If there are rep events
i_{a}
,
i_{s}
,
r
, and for every
j\in\overline{u}
, there is
(a_{j},r)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{pt}_{j}}
,
such that,

•

i_{a}=\textit{ralloc}(desc,\textsc{dptr})
with code line
32
,
where
{desc}.\textit{entries}=\overline{u}
.

•

i_{s}=\textit{ralloc}(\textsc{Undec},\textsc{cptr})
with code line
30
,

•

If
T
is the thread that invoked
x
, then
T
executes
i_{a}
and
i_{s}
within the invocation of
x
,

•

a_{j}=\textit{rdcss}(rD)
, where
rD=\textsc{rdesc}({i_{s}}.\textit{out},\ {pt}_{j},\ \textsc{Undec},\ {exp}_{j},\ {i_{a}}.\textit{out})
with code
line
51
,

•

r=\textit{rCAS}({i_{s}}.\textit{out},\textsc{Undec},\textsc{Succ})
with code
line
40
,

•

a_{j}
is the most recent such
rdcss
call for pointer
{pt}_{j}
before
r
.

then

\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}(\{(a_{j},r)\mid j\in\overline{u}\},\ true)
.

(b)

If there are rep events
i_{a}
,
i_{s}
,
r
and for some
j\in\overline{u}
, there is
(c_{j})\in\mathcal{S}_{{pt}_{j}}
,
such that for every

, there are
(a_{k},r)\in\mathcal{S}_{{pt}_{k}}
, such that,

•

i_{a}=\textit{ralloc}(desc,\textsc{dptr})
with code line

,
where
{desc}.\textit{entries}=\overline{u}
.

•

i_{s}=\textit{ralloc}(\textsc{Undec},\textsc{cptr})
with code line

,

•

If
T
is the thread that invoked
x
, then
T
executes
i_{a}
and
i_{s}
within the invocation of
x
,

•

a_{k}=\textit{rdcss}(rD)
, where

with code
line

, and

.

•

with code
line

,

•

a_{k}
is the most recent such

call for pointer
{pt}_{k}
before
r
.

•

, where

with code
line

, and
{c_{j}}.\textit{out}\neq{exp}_{j}
, and
\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}({c_{j}}.\textit{out})
.

•

was executed by the same thread that executed

, and
c_{j}
is the most recent such
rdcss
call for pointer

before

,

then

\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}(\{(a_{k},r),\ (c_{j})\mid k\in\overline{u}\wedge k<j\},\ false)
.

(c)

Otherwise,
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}(\emptyset,\bot)
.

2.

Case
x=\textit{mread}(p)
.

(a)

If there is

such that,

•

,

.

(b)

.

3.

Case

.

(a)

If there are

and rep event

, then

,

,

•

, then

\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}(\{(c)\},\ tt)
.

(b)

\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}(\emptyset,\bot)
.

4.

Case
x=\textit{malloc}(v)
.

(a)

If
{x}.\textit{out}\neq\bot
,
{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
, and
there is
(i)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{x}.\textit{out}}
such that,

•

If

, then

within the invocation of

,

•

with code line

,

then
