Lemma C.26
.

Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::postcondition-predicate-holds}}
holds.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
. We see from definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
that all non-empty cases have
o({x})\neq\bot
.
To prove
\mathcal{Q}_{{x},{o({x})}}
, we do a case analysis on
x
.

•

Case
x=\textit{mcas}(\overline{u})
. We want to prove,

\displaystyle o({x})\in\textsc{Bool}\wedge\begin{cases}\forall i\in\overline{u}.\ \exists z.\ {z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}{x}\wedge\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {{pt}_{i}}\ {{exp}_{i}}&\text{ if }o({x})=true\\
\begin{aligned} \exists i\in\overline{u},v\in\textsc{Val}.\ \exists z.\ {z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}{x}\wedge{}\\
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {{pt}_{i}}\ {v}\wedge v\neq{{exp}_{i}}\end{aligned}&\text{ if }o({x})=false\end{cases}

That
o({x})\in\textsc{Bool}
follows directly from definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
.
We now do a case analysis.

–

Case
o({x})=true
.
The success denotation has the form
\{(a_{i},r)\mid i\in\overline{u}\}
, where
r

is the event that sets the descriptor status to
Succ
and
each
a_{i}
writes the descriptor into
{pt}_{i}

(which means that each
a_{i}
reads the value
{exp}_{i}
).
Therefore, by Lemma
C.19
,
for every
i\in\overline{u}
,
there is
z
such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {{pt}_{i}}\ {{exp}_{i}}
.

–

Case
o({x})=false
.
The failing denotation has the form
\{(a_{k},r),\ (c_{i})\mid k\in\overline{u}\wedge k<i\}
, where
r

is the event that sets the descriptor status to
Fail
and
each
a_{k}
writes the descriptor into
{pt}_{k}

(which means that each
a_{k}
reads the value
{exp}_{k}
), but

c_{i}
failed to write the descriptor (so
c_{i}
read a value different from
{exp}_{i}
).
Therefore, by Lemma
C.19
,
there is
z
such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {{pt}_{i}}\ {v}
for some
v\neq{exp}_{i}
.

•

Case
x=\textit{malloc}(v)
. We want to prove
o({x})={x}.\textit{out}

and
{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
. But this follows trivially from the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
.

•

Case
x=\textit{mwrite}(q,v)
. We want to prove
o({x})=tt

but this follows trivially from the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
.

•

Case
x=\textit{rread}(q)
. We want to prove,

\displaystyle\exists z.\ {z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}{x}\wedge\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {q}\ {o({x})}

The only case in the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket

has a span
b
such that
f({b})
reads value
o({x})

in pointer
q
at line
10
.
Therefore, by Lemma
C.19
,
there is
z
such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {q}\ {o({x})}
.

∎

Lemma C.27
.

Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::writers-have-writer-blocks}}
holds.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
. We do a case analysis on
x
.

•

Case
x=\textit{mcas}(\overline{u})
.

\Longrightarrow
. By definition of
\mathcal{W}_{p}
, we have
p={pt}_{j}
for some
j\in\overline{u}
and,

\displaystyle\forall i\in\overline{u}.\ \exists z.\ {z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}{x}\wedge\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {{pt}_{i}}\ {{exp}_{i}}

(24)

By Lemma
C.19
, for every
i\in\overline{u}
, there are

b_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{{pt}_{i}}(x)
such that

f({b_{i}})
reads value
{exp}_{i}
.

The only case in the denotation that matches this conditions is (1).(a), i.e.,
the successful denotation. In particular, for
j\in\overline{u}
,

b_{j}\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{pt}_{j}}
.

\Longleftarrow
. Suppose
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.
The only applicable case in the denotation is the successful one (1).(a).
Hence,
b
must be of the form
(a_{j},r)
, for some
j\in\overline{u}
, where
a_{j}
writes the descriptor into

{pt}_{j}
. Hence,
p={pt}_{j}
.

Also, since the denotation contains all spans of the form
(a_{i},r)
for every
i\in\overline{u}
, and each
a_{i}

is a successful
rdcss
that reads the expected value
{exp}_{i}
,
(
24
) follows
by Lemma
C.19
.

•

Case
x=\textit{malloc}(v)
.

\Longrightarrow
. Since
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
, by definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
,

{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
,
{x}.\textit{out}\neq\bot
, and there is
a span in
\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{x}.\textit{out}}
.
But since
x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
, we have
p={x}.\textit{out}
.

\Longleftarrow
. Let
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.
By definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
,
{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
,
{x}.\textit{out}\neq\bot
,

b=(i)
for some alloc
i
at line

66
, and
(i)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{x}.\textit{out}}
.

But from
(i)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
we also know that
i
allocates
p
,
and since allocations are unique,
p={x}.\textit{out}
. Hence,
x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
.

•

Case
x=\textit{mwrite}(q,v)
.

\Longrightarrow
. Since
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
, there is
a span in
\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{q}
because all
spans in the denotation write in
q
. But
p=q
follows from

x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
.

\Longleftarrow
. Suppose
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
. Then,
p=q

since the same rep event writes into
p
(by definition of
\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
)
and into
q
(by definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
), which means
x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
.

•

Case
x=\textit{mread}(q)
. Trivial since
x
is neither a successful writer
nor it has writer spans in its denotation.

∎

Lemma C.28
.

Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::blocks-contained-in-abstract-time-interval}}
holds.

Proof.

We prove each item.

•

(i).
Let us focus on all denotations in
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
having
the form
\{(b)\}
.
In these denotations,
b
is a rep event
that is invoked by the same thread that invoked
x
. Hence,
b
starts after
x

started.

Now, let us focus on
\textit{mcas}(\overline{u})
. First, case (1).(a), which produces a denotation of the form

\{(a_{j},r)\mid j\in\overline{u}\}
. Rep event
a_{j}
can only
execute after
x
was started because line
51
executes
after the unique id was created by line
32
, which is executed
by the thread invoking
x
. The reasoning for case (1).(b) is similar.

•

(ii).
Let us focus on all denotations in
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
having
the form
\{(b)\}
.
In these denotations,
b
is a rep event
that is invoked by the same thread that invoked
x
. Hence,
b
finishes before
x

finishes (hence, in this cases, choose
i\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}b
).

Now, let us focus on
\textit{mcas}(\overline{u})
. First, case (1).(a), which produces a denotation of the form

\{(a_{j},r)\mid j\in\overline{u}\}
. Let
T
be the thread that invoked
x
. Before finishing

mcas
, thread
T
will have to execute line
44
.
Therefore, we can choose
i
to be this line, since this line executes before

x
finishes. Also, no thread can reach line
44

unless the status pointer
was changed to either
Succ
or
Fail
at line

40
, i.e., rep event
r
(notice the
if
at line

38
). The reasoning for case (1).(b) is similar.

∎

Lemma C.29
.

Axiom
S_{\ref{principle::containment-and-uniqueness-of-alloc-blocks}}
holds.

Proof.

If
b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
,
then by definition of
\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
,

b_{1}
and
b_{2}
are 1-tuples allocating
p
by executing
line
28
.
But by Lemma

C.17
,

b_{1}=b_{2}
.
∎

Lemma C.30
.

Axiom

S_{\ref{principle::every-block-must-have-an-allocated-pointer}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
b\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
.
Each procedure requires that its input pointers be created by a previous alloc invocation.
Therefore, there is some rep alloc
i
that allocates
p
and executes
line
28
. So that

i
executes before
f({b})
(in case
b
does not contain an alloc), or
b=(i)
.

Now, any rep event which is an instance of line
28

belongs to some span by definition of
\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
.
Therefore,
(i)\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
.

So, if
i
executes before
f({b})
, then
{(i)}\sqsubset^{S}{b}
, and if

b=(i)
, then
{(i)}\sqsubseteq^{S}{b}
.
∎

Lemma C.31
.

Axioms

S_{\ref{principle::allocs-have-alloc-blocks}}
holds.

Proof.

We prove each direction.

\Longrightarrow
. Since
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
and
x\in\mathcal{A}_{p}
then
x
is an alloc and

{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
,
{x}.\textit{out}\neq\bot
, and
p={x}.\textit{out}
. But by the definition of the denotation,
there is
(i)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{x}.\textit{out}}
, where
i
is line

28
.
Therefore,
(i)\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
by definition.

\Longleftarrow
. Let
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
.
By definition of
\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}
,
f({b})
allocates
p
and it is
line
28
.
But the only case applicable in the denotation is when
x
is an alloc, which means

{x}.\textit{end}\neq\bot
,
{x}.\textit{out}\neq\bot
and
b\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{x}.\textit{out}}
.
Also, since rep allocs are unique
p={x}.\textit{out}
, which
means
x\in\mathcal{A}_{p}
.
∎

Theorem C.3
.

V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})

is implemented by
span structure
S^{\textsc{M}}
.

Proof.

All span axioms hold from Lemma
C.20
to
Lemma
C.31
.
∎

Theorem C.4
.

The MCAS implementation of Figure
14
is linearizable.

Proof.

By Theorem
A.1
, it
suffices to show that

V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})
is valid. But by
Theorem
B.1
, it suffices that

V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})
is implemented by span
structure
S^{\textsc{M}}
. This is given by Theorem

C.3
.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Validity and Proof of Opportunism Axioms for MCAS with Opportunistic Readers

The relations in Definition
B.3

suffice to verify RDCSS and MCAS with standard
helping implementations.
However, in the case of MCAS with opportunistic
mread
, we need
to consider new cases in order to capture the new opportunistic
strategy of the readers. To motivate the new definitions for the visibility relations,
consider Figure
15
. Black spans in the figure are the standard
descriptor spans (henceforth, d-spans), which we treated in Section
5
with the name spans; while
gray spans are opportunistic spans (henceforth, o-spans), which are the new kind of spans
we will introduce in this section.

Figure 15
:
Two executions of opportunistic
mread
event
A
and
mcas
event
B
.

A
executes fully within the time extension of
B
.
Black spans are descriptor spans, while the gray span is an opportunistic
span.
Spans
a
and
b
are named for later reference.

Figure
15
shows a typical interaction
between an opportunistic
mread
(event
A
) and an
mcas

(event
B
). Event
A
executes fully during the execution of
B
.
The gray span
a
in
A
overlaps with span
b
in
B
. Both
a
and

b
access the same pointer. Span
a
is painted gray to emphasize
that it is a different kind of span: it does not involve writing a
descriptor, but is, rather, the extension of time between discovering
a descriptor at line
2
in
Figure
4(b)
and then observing the descriptor status
at line
5
. For this example,
we will assume that the black spans finish when the descriptor
status is set to
Succ
at line
14
in Figure

3
.

Even though spans
a
and
b
overlap in Figure

15
, there is still a sense in which we
can separate
A
before
B
. In the figure,
a
observed the descriptor status
before the descriptor was resolved. Therefore, line
5
in
Figure
4(b)
returned
Undec
,
which implies that
mread
returned the expected value, as if the
descriptor was not present. In other words,
A
missed the effect
of
B
, and therefore
A
can be
separated
before
B
.
Another way in which we can separate
A
before
B
is if
A
attempted
to discover the descriptor before
B
wrote its descriptor.
These two modes of separation can be expressed by the relation

{A}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{B}

which reads “
A
is
opportunistically separable-before
B
at pointer
p
”, and it is defined as:

{A}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{B}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists x\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(A),y\in\mathcal{S}(B)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}.\ ({f({x})}<^{R}{f({y})}\vee{l({x})}<^{R}{l({y})})

Here,
\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(A)
is
the set of o-spans in
A
accessing pointer
p
(i.e., the gray spans in
A
);

\mathcal{S}(B)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
the set of successful
p
-writer d-spans in
B
;

{f({x})}<^{R}{f({y})}

means that the first rep event of span
x
executes before the first rep event
of span
y
(i.e.,
x
attempted to discover a descriptor before
y

wrote a descriptor); and

{l({x})}<^{R}{l({y})}
means that
the last rep event executed by span
x
finished before the last
rep event executed by span
y
(i.e.,
x
observed the descriptor status
before
y
resolved it).
The “gap” between
A
and
B
in this case
is not a gap in real time as it was in the helpers case, but is
rather
logical
.

Analogous to how we introduced an observation relation paired with the
separable-before relation in Section
3.3
,
we next introduce an observation relation for

\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}
.

In Figure
15
,
A
missed the effect of

B
because
a
observed the descriptor status before
b
resolved
the descriptor to
Succ
.
Therefore, if
A
observes the descriptor status
after
the
descriptor’s resolution by
b
,
A
will not miss the effect of
B

(indeed,
A
returns the
new value stored in the descriptor when line

5
in Figure

4(b)
returns
Succ
, as if the
write to the pointer already occurred). This suggests that we should
define that
A
observes
B
at
p
, if
B
executed the most recent
successful
p
-writer span that resolved the descriptor before
A

read the status (and also, if
B
wrote the descriptor before
A
attempted to
discover it, otherwise
A
would not discover a descriptor). We can express
this by the relation
{B}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{A}

which reads “
B
is
opportunistically observed by
A
at pointer
p
”, and it is defined as:

{B}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{A}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists x\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(B),y\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(A).\ x=\max_{\sqsubseteq^{S}}\{z\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\mid{f({z})}<^{R}{f({y})}\wedge{l({z})}<^{R}{l({y})}\}

Here,
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(B)
is the set of
p
-accessing d-spans in
B
and

\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
is the set of
p
-writing d-spans. Notice that

{f({z})}<^{R}{f({y})}
captures that
z
wrote the descriptor
before
y
attempted to discover it, and

{l({z})}<^{R}{l({y})}
captures that
z
resolved before
y
read the status.
Also,
\sqsubseteq^{S}
is the span returns-before relation of Section
5
,
which still linearizes
p
-accessing descriptor spans, and hence, taking the maximum
under
\sqsubseteq^{S}
makes sense.

Since descriptor spans are still present, we need to keep the visibility relations
we used for the standard implementations in Definition
B.3
,
we just tag the relations with a superscript
to indicate that these apply over descriptor spans,

\displaystyle{A}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{B}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists x\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(A),y\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(B).\ {x}\sqsubset^{S}{y}

\displaystyle{A}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{B}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists x\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(A),y\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(B).\ x=\max_{\sqsubseteq^{S}}\{z\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\mid{z}\sqsubset^{S}{y}\}

Finally, our visibility relations will be the union of the opportunistic and standard components,

\displaystyle\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}}\cup{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}}\cup{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}}

As we did with spans in Section
5
,
we will introduce an abstract notion of opportunistic
span via opportunistic structures.
We will state axioms that opportunistic structures need to satisfy, which we call opportunism axioms
(Section
D.1
).
The opportunism axioms are a superset of the span axioms of Section
5
, extending
the invariants of descriptor spans with invariants about opportunistic spans.
The opportunistic structures and opportunism axioms will allow us to split the validity proof
of
V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})
into two parts.
The first part of the proof concludes the validity of
V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})

from the assumption that some opportunistic structure satisfies the opportunism axioms (Section
D.2
).
The second part of the proof defines an opportunistic structure for opportunistic MCAS and shows that
this structure satisfies the opportunism axioms (Section
D.3
).
Together with Theorem
A.2
,
we then conclude that opportunistic MCAS is linearizable (Theorem
D.3
).

D.1
Key Concepts

As we did in Section
5
,
we suppose a linearization
\leq^{R}

over the set of rep events. We also denote as
[\leq^{R}]
the domain
of
\leq^{R}
.

Definition D.1

(Opportunistic Structure)
.

A
opportunistic structure

(\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)
consists
of:

•

For every pointer
p
, a set
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}\subseteq\textsc{Rep}\times\textsc{Rep}
, called the
descriptor spans
accessing
p
.

•

For every pointer
p
, a set
\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}\subseteq\textsc{Rep}\times\textsc{Rep}
, called the
opportunistic spans
accessing
p
.

•

For every pointer
p
, a set
\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
, called the
successful write spans
into
p
.

•

For every pointer
p
, a set
\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
, called the
alloc spans
creating
p
.

•

A function
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket:\textsc{Ev}\rightarrow(\mathcal{P}(\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p})\times(\textsc{Val}\cup\{\bot\}))
,
called the
event denotation
, written
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
for event
x
. Here,
\mathcal{P}(\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p})
denotes the power set of
\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
.

where
\mathcal{S}_{p}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}}\cup{\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}}
.

Opportunistic structures are like the span structures of Definition
B.1
.
The only differences are that there is a new set
\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}
modeling the opportunistic spans, and
what previously was the set of spans
\mathcal{S}_{p}
is now the set
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
of descriptor spans.
The set of spans
\mathcal{S}_{p}
now refers to the union of
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
and
\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}
.
Each span in
\mathcal{S}_{p}
is a pair of rep events. We denote as

f
(“first”) and
l
(“last”)
the standard pair projection functions. We write spans of the form

(a,a)
as
(a)
.

For o-span
b
, it is useful to think of
f({b})
as the rep event that discovers the descriptor, and

l({b})
as the rep event that read the descriptor status. For d-span
b
, we can still think of

f({b})
as the rep event that writes the descriptor, and
l({b})
as the rep event that resolves the descriptor.

Definition D.2

(Notation for Opportunistic Structures)
.

Given opportunistic structure

(\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)
,
we make the following definitions.

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{D}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}

(Descriptor spans)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{O}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}

(Oportunistic spans)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\pi_{1}({\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket})

(Spans in the denotation of
x
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{p}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}_{p}

(
p
-accessing spans in the denotation of
x
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}

(
p
-accessing descriptor spans in the denotation of
x
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}

(
p
-accessing opportunistic spans in the denotation of
x
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{D}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{D}

(Descriptor spans in the denotation of
x
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{O}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{O}

(Oportunistic spans in the denotation of
x
)

\displaystyle o({x})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\pi_{2}({\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket})

(Denotation output of
x
)

Definition D.3

(Span Relations)
.

Given opportunistic structure

(\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)
,
we define the following relation over
\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
,

\displaystyle{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{l({b})}<^{R}{f({c})}

We now define the notion of implementation by an opportunistic structure,

Definition D.4

(Implementation by an opportunistic structure)
.

Given arbitrary relations

\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
,

\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}
, we say that

V(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})=(\mathcal{W}_{p},\mathcal{A}_{p},\mathcal{Q})
is
implemented by opportunistic
structure

(\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)
if all
the opportunism axioms in Figure
16
are
satisfied. We also say that

V(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})=(\mathcal{W}_{p},\mathcal{A}_{p},\mathcal{Q})
is
implemented opportunistically

if there exists some opportunistic structure that implements it.

Span returns-before relation

{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{l({b})}<^{R}{f({c})}

Set of
p
-accessing spans

\mathcal{S}_{p}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}}\cup{\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}}

Denotation output

o({x})\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\pi_{2}({\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket})

Set of spans in a denotation

\mathcal{S}(x)\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\pi_{1}({\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket})

Set of
p
-accessing descriptor spans in a denotation

\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x)\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}

Set of
p
-accessing opportunistic spans in a denotation

\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(x)\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}

Opportunistic access predicate

\mathsf{OpAcc}\ {b}\ {x}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists p\in\textsc{pts},c\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}.

\mathsf{OpAcc}\ {b}\ {x}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}

b\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}\wedge f({c})\leq^{R}l({b})\leq^{R}l({c})

(a)

Defined notions

(
O_{1}
) Disjointness

b,c\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}\implies({{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}}\vee{{c}\sqsubset^{S}{b}}\vee{b=c})

(
O_{2}
) Bunching

b,c\in\mathcal{S}(x)\implies{f({b})}\leq^{R}{l({c})}

(
O_{3}
) Opportunism

\mathsf{OpAcc}\ {b}\ {x}\implies\forall c\in\mathcal{S}(x).\ {l({b})}<^{R}{l({c})}

(
O_{4}
) Adequacy

x\in T\implies o({x})={x}.\textit{out}\wedge\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset

(b)

Key Axioms

(
O_{5}
)

{f({b})}\leq^{R}{l({b})}

(
O_{6}
)

\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}\cap\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}=\emptyset

(
O_{7}
)

\mathcal{S}_{p}\cap\mathcal{S}_{q}\neq\emptyset\implies p=q

(
O_{8}
)

b,c\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}\implies b=c

(
O_{9}
)

b\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\implies\exists c\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}.\ {c}\sqsubseteq^{S}{b}

(
O_{10}
)

\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}(y)\neq\emptyset\implies x=y

(
O_{11}
)

b\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\implies\exists x.\ b\in\mathcal{S}(x)

(
O_{12}
)

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset\implies o({x})\neq\bot\wedge\mathcal{Q}_{{x},{o({x})}}

(
O_{13}
)

\neg\exists b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p},c\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}.\ f({b})=f({c})

(
O_{14}
)

(\mathcal{S}(x)\subseteq\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p})\vee(\mathcal{S}(x)\subseteq\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p})

(
O_{15}
)

(b\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(x)\wedge c\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{q}(x))\implies b=c

(
O_{16}
)

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset\implies(x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\neq\emptyset)

(
O_{17}
)

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset\implies(x\in\mathcal{A}_{p}\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}\neq\emptyset)

(
O_{18}
)

(i)
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\implies{x}.\textit{start}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{f({b})}.\textit{start}

(ii)
(b\in\mathcal{S}(x)\wedge x\in T)\implies{}

\exists i\in[\leq^{R}].\ {i}.\textit{end}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{x}.\textit{end}\wedge{l({b})}\leq^{R}{i}

(c)

Structural Axioms

Figure 16
:
Opportunism axioms for
(\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)
implementing
V(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})=(\mathcal{W}_{p},\mathcal{A}_{p},\mathcal{Q})
. Variables
x
,
y
range over
Ev
. Variables
b
,
c
over
\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
. Variables
p
,
q
over
pts
.

We now explain the axioms. Since the opportunism axioms in Figure
16

extend the span axioms of Figure
12
, we will explain only the new axioms.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunistic-access}}
makes use of the opportunistic access predicate

\mathsf{OpAcc}\ {b}\ {x}
, which states that
b
is an o-span that read the descriptor status of some descriptor
written by event
x
, where the status read by
b
happens after the descriptor was written but before the descriptor is
successfully resolved,
i.e.,
f({c})\leq^{R}l({b})\leq^{R}l({c})
.
If
\mathsf{OpAcc}\ {b}\ {x}
happens, we say that
b
opportunistically accesses
x
.
Hence, the axiom states that if
b
opportunistically accesses
x
, then the status read must
have happened before
any
descriptor resolution carried out by
x
. For MCAS this holds, because
all spans in an
mcas
event simultaneously resolve the descriptor at line
14
in
Figure
3
,
which means that any opportunistic access before line
14
will happen
before the resolution of all the spans in the
mcas
event.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-opor-spans-disjoint}}
states that there a no
p
-accessing spans that
are both a d-span and an o-span, i.e., d-spans and o-spans are disjoint.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::spans-access-at-most-one-pointer}}
states that spans access a unique pointer.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::different-span-kinds-do-not-share-reps}}
states that there is no
p
-accessing
d-spans and o-spans that share their first rep event.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::runs-have-at-most-one-type-of-span}}
states that denotations contain only one kind of span,
i.e., you either help or are opportunistic in the entire execution.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
states that opportunistic accesses are unique inside a denotation.

We now state the visibility relations we will use. These are just the relations discussed while
explaining Figure
15
.

Definition D.5

(Visibility Relations for Opportunism)
.

Given opportunistic structure

(\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)
,
we define the pointer-indexed separable-before
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}
and
observation
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
relations for implementations having opportunism,

\displaystyle\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}}\cup{\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}}\cup{\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}}

where,

\displaystyle{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{y}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x),c\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(y).\ {b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}

\displaystyle{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{y}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x),c\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(y).\ b=\max_{\sqsubseteq^{S}}\{d\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\mid{d}\sqsubset^{S}{c}\}

\displaystyle{x}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{y}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists b\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(x),c\in\mathcal{S}(y)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}.\ ({f({b})}<^{R}{f({c})}\vee{l({b})}<^{R}{l({c})})

\displaystyle{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{y}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x),c\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(y).\ b=\max_{\sqsubseteq^{S}}\{d\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\mid{f({d})}<^{R}{f({c})}\wedge{l({d})}<^{R}{l({c})}\}

Definition D.6

(Visibility relations shorthands)
.

We also introduce the following shorthands. These are technical definitions that some lemmas will use,

\displaystyle\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\bigcup_{p}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\bigcup_{p}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{D}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\bigcup_{p}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{D}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\bigcup_{p}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}

D.2
Proof of Validity

Definition D.7

(Section Hypotheses)
.

To shorten the statements of propositions from
D.1

to
D.1
, the
following hypotheses will apply,

•

Ev
is an arbitrary set of events.

•

Rep
is an arbitrary set of rep events, linearized under a given
\leq^{R}
.

•

Z\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}(\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p},\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p},\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket)

is an arbitrary opportunistic structure.

•

V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})

is implemented by
Z
, where relations
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
,
\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}

are those in Definition
D.5
and instantiated with

Z
.

The first lemmas describe basic results whose statements are
self-explanatory.

Lemma D.1
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
Relation
\sqsubset^{S}
defines a strict partial order on the set

\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
.

Proof.

Each required property follows,

•

Irreflexivity. Suppose for a contradiction that

{a}\sqsubset^{S}{a}
for some
a\in\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
.
Hence,
l({a})<^{R}f({a})
by
definition.
But by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

{f({a})}\leq^{R}{l({a})}
.
Therefore,
{l({a})}<^{R}{l({a})}

(Contradiction).

•

Transitivity. Let
{a}\sqsubset^{S}{b}
and

{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
.

We have by definition that

l({a})<^{R}f({b})
and

l({b})<^{R}f({c})
.
But by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

{f({b})}\leq^{R}{l({b})}
.
Therefore,
{l({a})}<^{R}{f({c})}
,
which means
{a}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
.

∎

Lemma D.2
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
Relation
\sqsubseteq^{S}
defines a partial order on the set

\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}
. In addition, for every
q
,
relation
\sqsubseteq^{S}
defines a linear order on the set

\mathcal{S}^{D}_{q}
.

Proof.

By Lemma

D.1
,

\sqsubset^{S}
defines a strict partial order. It is a
standard result that the reflexive closure of a strict partial
order defines a partial order (i.e. a reflexive, transitive,
and antisymmetric relation).

Since for every
q
,
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{q}\subseteq\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}_{p}

holds, relation
\sqsubseteq^{S}
is also a partial order
on
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{q}
for every
q
.
That
\sqsubseteq^{S}
is a linear order
for
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{q}
is simply Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
.
∎

Therefore, Lemma
D.2

implies that it makes sense to take a maximum under
\sqsubseteq^{S}

in Definition
D.5
.

The next lemma states that denotations can have at most one
p
-accessing d-span.

Lemma D.3
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
If
b,c\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x)
, then
b=c
.

Proof.

By Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
,
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
or
{c}\sqsubset^{S}{b}
or

b=c
.

If
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
, since they are in the same denotation, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({b})

Similarly, if
{c}\sqsubset^{S}{b}
, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

l({c})<^{R}f({b})\leq^{R}l({c})

Hence,
b=c
.
∎

The next lemma states that there is no span that is both a descriptor span and
a opportunistic span at the same time. It also states that denotations have only one kind
of span.

Lemma D.4
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
. We have the following facts,

1.

\mathcal{S}^{D}\cap\mathcal{S}^{O}=\emptyset
holds.

2.

For any event
x
, it is not the case that both
\mathcal{S}^{D}(x)\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}^{O}(x)\neq\emptyset

hold simultaneously.

3.

If
\mathcal{S}^{O}(x)\neq\emptyset
, then
\mathcal{S}(x)\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{O}
.

4.

If
\mathcal{S}^{D}(x)\neq\emptyset
, then
\mathcal{S}(x)\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{D}
.

Proof.

We prove each item as follows,

1.

Suppose for a contradiction that
b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}\cap\mathcal{S}^{O}
.
By definition,
b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}\cap\mathcal{S}^{O}_{q}
, for some

p
and
q
. But by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::spans-access-at-most-one-pointer}}
,
p=q
holds,
which contradicts Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-opor-spans-disjoint}}
.

2.

Suppose for a contradiction that
b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}(x)
and
c\in\mathcal{S}^{O}(x)
.
By Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::runs-have-at-most-one-type-of-span}}
, we have the cases,

•

Case
\mathcal{S}(x)\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{D}
. Therefore,
c\in\mathcal{S}^{D}
also.
But
c\in\mathcal{S}^{D}\cap\mathcal{S}^{O}
contradicts part (1).

•

Case
\mathcal{S}(x)\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{O}
. Therefore,
b\in\mathcal{S}^{O}
also.
But
b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}\cap\mathcal{S}^{O}
contradicts part (1).

3.

Let
b\in\mathcal{S}^{O}(x)
and
c\in\mathcal{S}(x)
. We know
c\in\mathcal{S}^{D}\cup\mathcal{S}^{O}
.
If
c\in\mathcal{S}^{D}
, then we have simultaneously
\mathcal{S}^{D}(x)\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}^{O}(x)\neq\emptyset

which contradicts part (2).

4.

Similar to the previous item.

∎

For the next two lemmas, we will use the following relation,

Definition D.8
.

Define,

{\leftslice}\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{D}}}\cup{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{D}}}\cup{({\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}\circ{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}})}

where
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}\circ{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}

is the relation composition of
\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}
and
\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}
.

The next lemma states that events related under the transitive closure of
\leftslice
must contain disjoint spans.

Lemma D.5
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
If
x\mathrel{{\leftslice}^{+}}y
then
\exists b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x),b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(y).\ {b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.

Proof.

Define the following binary relation on
Ev
,

P(w,z)\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\exists b_{w}\in\mathcal{S}(w),b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}(z).\ {b_{w}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}

So, we need to show
x\mathrel{{\leftslice}^{+}}y\implies P(x,y)
.
But it suffices to show the following two properties,

•

{\leftslice}\subseteq P
.

•

P
is transitive.

because
\mathrel{{\leftslice}^{+}}
is the smallest transitive
relation containing
\leftslice
.

Let us show the two required properties.

•

{\leftslice}\subseteq P
.

The cases for
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{D}}}
and
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{D}}}
are immediate from
their definition. Let us focus on
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}\circ{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
.

So, let
x\mathrel{({\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}\circ{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}})}y
. Hence,

{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{z}
and
{z}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{q}}^{\mathsf{O}}{y}

for some
p
,
q
, and
z
.

By definition, there are
b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x)
,
c\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(z)
,
d\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{q}(z)
,
e\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{q}(y)
such that

{l({b})}<^{R}{l({c})}
and
{f({b})}<^{R}{f({c})}
and (
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({e})}
or
{f({d})}<^{R}{f({e})}
).

By Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
,
c=d
. Hence,
c\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\cap\mathcal{S}_{q}
, which implies
p=q
by
Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::spans-access-at-most-one-pointer}}
.

Since
b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x)
and
e\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(y)
, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
there are
three cases,

–

Case
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{e}
. This is the required conclusion.

–

Case
{e}\sqsubset^{S}{b}
. If
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({e})}
, then together with Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
, we get the following contradiction,

l({e})<^{R}f({b})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}l({c})=l({d})<^{R}l({e})

If
{f({d})}<^{R}{f({e})}
, then together with Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
, we get the following contradiction,

f({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({e})<^{R}f({b})<^{R}f({c})=f({d})

–

Case
e=b
. If
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({e})}
, we get the following contradiction,

l({e})=l({b})<^{R}l({c})=l({d})<^{R}l({e})

If
{f({d})}<^{R}{f({e})}
, we get the following contradiction,

f({e})=f({b})<^{R}f({c})=f({d})<^{R}f({e})

•

P
is transitive. By hypotheses
P(u,v)
and
P(v,z)
,
we have,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\exists b_{u}\in\mathcal{S}(u),b_{v}\in\mathcal{S}(v).\ {b_{u}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{v}}\\
\exists b_{v}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}(v),b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}(z).\ {b_{v}^{\prime}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}\end{split}

(25)

We need to show
P(u,z)
. By the bunching axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}

on denotation
\mathcal{S}(v)
,

{f({b_{v}})}\leq^{R}{l({b_{v}^{\prime}})}
must hold. Hence,

l({b_{u}})<^{R}f({b_{v}})\leq^{R}l({b_{v}^{\prime}})<^{R}f({b_{z}})

which means
{b_{u}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}
.

∎

Using
\leftslice
, we can now extract some information regarding the transitive closure of
\prec
.

Lemma D.6
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
If
{x}\prec^{+}{y}
, then
x\neq y
and
\exists b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x),b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(y).\ {f({b_{x}})}<^{R}{l({b_{y}})}
.

Proof.

Notice that by definition of
\prec
, we can see it as the union of the four relations,

\displaystyle{\prec}={\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{D}}}\cup{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{D}}}\cup{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}\cup{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}

(26)

Hence, from
{x}\prec^{+}{y}
, we have a sequence of length
n\geq_{\mathbb{N}}1
,

a_{0}R_{1}a_{1}R_{2}\ldots R_{n-1}a_{n-1}R_{n}a_{n}

where each
R_{i}
is one of the four relations in (
26
),
and
a_{0}=x
and
a_{n}=y
.

Claim
.

Let
1\leq_{\mathbb{N}}i<_{\mathbb{N}}n
. If
R_{i+1}={\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
, then
R_{i}={\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
.

We now prove the claim. Suppose
{a_{i}}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{a_{i+1}}
holds for some
p
.
We do a case analysis on
R_{i}
and show that all the cases different from
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
lead
to a contradiction.

•

R_{i}={\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{D}}}
or
R_{i}={\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{D}}}
. Hence, by definition,

\mathcal{S}^{D}(a_{i})\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}^{O}(a_{i})\neq\emptyset
, which contradicts Lemma
D.4
.

•

R_{i}={\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
. Again, by definition,

\mathcal{S}^{D}(a_{i})\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}^{O}(a_{i})\neq\emptyset
, which contradicts Lemma
D.4
.

This proves the claim.

Claim
.

Let
1\leq_{\mathbb{N}}i<_{\mathbb{N}}n
. If
R_{i}={\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
, then
R_{i+1}={\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
.

We now prove the claim. Suppose
{a_{i-1}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{a_{i}}
holds for some
p
.
We do a case analysis on
R_{i+1}
and show that all the cases different from
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
lead
to a contradiction.

•

R_{i+1}={\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{D}}}
or
R_{i+1}={\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{D}}}
. Hence, by definition,

\mathcal{S}^{O}(a_{i})\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}^{D}(a_{i})\neq\emptyset
, which contradicts Lemma
D.4
.

•

R_{i+1}={\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
. Again, by definition,

\mathcal{S}^{O}(a_{i})\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}^{D}(a_{i})\neq\emptyset
, which contradicts Lemma
D.4
.

This proves the claim.

By the above claims, relations
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
and
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
always
occur contiguously in the sequence, first
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
followed by
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
.
The only places where this could be violated is at the extremes of the sequence, i.e., at
R_{1}
and
R_{n}
, because at
R_{1}
, a
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
could appear without a preceding
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
, and at
R_{n}
, a
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
could appear without a succeeding
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
.

First, we case analyze when
n=1
.

•

Case
{a_{0}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{a_{1}}
or

{a_{0}}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{a_{1}}
(for some
p
).
By definition, there are
b\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(a_{0})
and
c\in\mathcal{S}_{p}(a_{1})
such that
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
.

Therefore, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

f({b})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({c})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{1}
. Then, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({b})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{1}
.

•

Case
{a_{0}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{a_{1}}
(for some
p
).
By definition, there are
b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(a_{0})
and
c\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(a_{1})
such that
{f({b})}<^{R}{f({c})}

and
{l({b})}<^{R}{l({c})}
.

Therefore, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

f({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({c})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{1}
. But then,

\mathcal{S}^{O}(a_{0})\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}^{D}(a_{0})\neq\emptyset
, which
contradicts Lemma
D.4
.

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{1}
.

•

Case
{a_{0}}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{a_{1}}
(for some
p
).
By definition, there are
b\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(a_{0})
and
c\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(a_{1})
such that (
{f({b})}<^{R}{f({c})}

or
{l({b})}<^{R}{l({c})}
).

If
{f({b})}<^{R}{f({c})}
, then by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

f({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({c})

Similarly, if
{l({b})}<^{R}{l({c})}
, then by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

f({b})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}l({c})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{1}
. But then,

\mathcal{S}^{O}(a_{0})\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}^{D}(a_{0})\neq\emptyset
, which
contradicts Lemma
D.4
.

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{1}
.

Now, let us focus when
n\geq_{\mathbb{N}}2
. We consider 4 cases on the extremes of the sequence.

•

Case
R_{1}\neq{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
and
R_{n}\neq{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
.
Then
a_{0}\mathrel{{\leftslice}^{+}}a_{n}
, since every occurrence of
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
is followed by
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
. Therefore, by Lemma
D.5
, there are
b\in\mathcal{S}(a_{0})
and
c\in\mathcal{S}(a_{n})
such that
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
.

Hence, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

f({b})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({c})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{n}
. Then, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({b})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{n}
.

•

Case
R_{1}\neq{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
and
R_{n}={\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
.
Then
a_{0}\mathrel{{\leftslice}^{+}}a_{n-1}
because
R_{n-1}
cannot be
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
(otherwise

R_{n}
would be
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
).

By Lemma
D.5
, there are
b\in\mathcal{S}(a_{0})
and
c\in\mathcal{S}(a_{n-1})

such that
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
. From
{a_{n-1}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{a_{n}}
(for some
p
), we have

d\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(a_{n-1})
and
e\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(a_{n})
such that
f({d})<^{R}f({e})
and
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({e})}
.
Together with Axioms
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
and
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,

f({b})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({d})<^{R}l({e})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{n}
. Then, by Lemma
D.4
,

b\in\mathcal{S}^{O}(a_{n})
, which means
b=e
by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
.
But then, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

l({d})<^{R}l({e})=l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({d})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{n}
.

•

Case
R_{1}={\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
and
R_{n}\neq{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
.
Then
a_{1}\mathrel{{\leftslice}^{+}}a_{n}
because
R_{2}
cannot be
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
(otherwise

R_{1}
would be
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
).

By Lemma
D.5
, there are
b\in\mathcal{S}(a_{1})
and
c\in\mathcal{S}(a_{n})

such that
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
. From
{a_{0}}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{a_{1}}
(for some
p
), we have

d\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(a_{0})
and
e\in\mathcal{S}(a_{1})\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
such that (
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({e})}
or
{f({d})}<^{R}{f({e})})
.

–

Case
{f({d})}<^{R}{f({e})}
. Then we get, together with Axioms

O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
and
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,

f({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({c})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{n}
. Then, by Lemma
D.4
,

c\in\mathcal{S}^{O}(a_{0})
, which means
c=d
by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
.
But then, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

f({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})=f({d})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{n}
.

–

Case
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({e})}
. Since rep events are totally ordered,
l({d})<^{R}f({e})
or
f({e})\leq^{R}l({d})
.

*

Case
l({d})<^{R}f({e})
. Together with Axioms

O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
and
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,

f({d})\leq^{R}l({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({c})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{n}
. Then, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

l({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({d})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{n}
.

*

Case
f({e})\leq^{R}l({d})
. Hence,
f({e})\leq^{R}l({d})<^{R}l({e})
,
which means
\mathsf{OpAcc}\ {d}\ {a_{1}}
must be true. So, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunistic-access}}
,
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({b})}
holds. Which means,
by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

f({d})\leq^{R}l({d})<^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({c})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{n}
. Then, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

l({d})<^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({d})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{n}
.

•

Case
R_{1}={\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
and
R_{n}={\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
.
Since this time we are removing
R_{1}
and
R_{n}
from the sequence, we need to take into account the special case when the sequence from

R_{2}
to
R_{n-1}
is empty (i.e.,
n=2
).

–

Case: The sequence from
R_{2}
to
R_{n-1}
is empty (equivalently,
n=2
).
From
{a_{0}}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{a_{1}}

and
{a_{1}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}^{\mathsf{O}}{a_{2}}
(for some
p
and
q
), there are
d\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(a_{0})
and
e\in\mathcal{S}(a_{1})\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
such that (
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({e})}
or
{f({d})}<^{R}{f({e})}
); and
b\in\mathcal{S}(a_{1})\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{q}
and
c\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{q}(a_{2})

such that
{l({b})}<^{R}{l({c})}
and
{f({b})}<^{R}{f({c})}
.

Since rep events are totally ordered,
l({d})<^{R}f({e})
or
f({e})\leq^{R}l({d})
.

*

Case
l({d})<^{R}f({e})
. Together with Axioms

O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
and
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,

f({d})\leq^{R}l({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}l({c})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{2}
. Then, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
,
c=d
. Hence, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

l({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}l({c})=l({d})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{2}
.

*

Case
f({e})\leq^{R}l({d})
. We have two subcases,

·

Case
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({e})}
. Hence,
f({e})\leq^{R}l({d})<^{R}l({e})
,
which means
\mathsf{OpAcc}\ {d}\ {a_{1}}
must be true. So, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunistic-access}}
,
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({b})}
holds. Which means,
by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

f({d})\leq^{R}l({d})<^{R}l({b})<^{R}l({c})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{2}
. Then, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
,
c=d
.
We get the contradiction,

l({d})<^{R}l({b})<^{R}l({c})=l({d})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{2}
.

·

Case
{f({d})}<^{R}{f({e})}
. Hence, together with Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,

f({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}l({c})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{2}
. Then, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
,
c=d
. Hence,
c\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\cap\mathcal{S}_{q}
, which implies
p=q
by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::spans-access-at-most-one-pointer}}
. But since
b,e\in\mathcal{S}(a_{1})\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
,
by Lemma
D.3
,
b=e
.
Therefore, we get the following contradiction,

f({b})<^{R}f({c})=f({d})<^{R}f({e})=f({b})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{2}
.

–

Case: The sequence from
R_{2}
to
R_{n-1}
is not empty (equivalently,
n\geq_{\mathbb{N}}3
).
Then
a_{1}\mathrel{{\leftslice}^{+}}a_{n-1}
because
R_{2}
cannot be
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
(otherwise

R_{1}
would be
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
) and
R_{n-1}
cannot be
{\mathrel{\lessdot}^{\mathsf{O}}}
(otherwise

R_{n}
would be
{\mathrel{\ltimes}^{\mathsf{O}}}
).

By Lemma
D.5
, there are
b\in\mathcal{S}(a_{1})
and
c\in\mathcal{S}(a_{n-1})

such that
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{c}
. From
{a_{0}}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{a_{1}}

and
{a_{n-1}}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}^{\mathsf{O}}{a_{n}}
(for some
p
and
q
), there are
d\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(a_{0})
and
e\in\mathcal{S}(a_{1})\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
such that (
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({e})}
or
{f({d})}<^{R}{f({e})}
); and
g\in\mathcal{S}(a_{n-1})\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{q}
and
h\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{q}(a_{n})
such that
{l({g})}<^{R}{l({h})}
and
{f({g})}<^{R}{f({h})}
.

Since rep events are totally ordered,
l({d})<^{R}f({e})
or
f({e})\leq^{R}l({d})
.

*

Case
l({d})<^{R}f({e})
. Together with Axioms

O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
and
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,

f({d})\leq^{R}l({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({g})<^{R}l({h})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{n}
. Then,
h=d
by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
. Hence, with Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

l({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({g})<^{R}l({h})=l({d})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{n}
.

*

Case
f({e})\leq^{R}l({d})
. We have two subcases,

·

Case
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({e})}
.
Hence,
f({e})\leq^{R}l({d})<^{R}l({e})
,
which means
\mathsf{OpAcc}\ {d}\ {a_{1}}
must be true. So, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunistic-access}}
,
{l({d})}<^{R}{l({b})}
holds. Which means,
by Axioms
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
and
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,

f({d})\leq^{R}l({d})<^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({g})<^{R}l({h})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{n}
. Then,
h=d
by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
. Hence, with Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,
we get the contradiction,

l({d})<^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({g})<^{R}l({h})=l({d})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{n}
.

·

Case
{f({d})}<^{R}{f({e})}
. Hence, together with Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
,

f({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({g})<^{R}l({h})

Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{0}=a_{n}
. Then, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
,
h=d
. Hence,
h\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\cap\mathcal{S}_{q}
, which implies
p=q
by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::spans-access-at-most-one-pointer}}
. Since
g,e\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
, we have three further subcases
by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
, the three of them leading to a contradiction,

If
{e}\sqsubset^{S}{g}
, then by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
, we have the contradiction,

f({g})<^{R}f({h})=f({d})<^{R}f({e})\leq^{R}l({e})<^{R}f({g})

If
{g}\sqsubset^{S}{e}
, then by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
, we have the contradiction,

f({e})\leq^{R}l({b})<^{R}f({c})\leq^{R}l({g})<^{R}f({e})

If
g=e
, then we have the contradiction,

f({e})=f({g})<^{R}f({h})=f({d})<^{R}f({e})

Hence,
a_{0}\neq a_{n}
.

∎

A direct consequence of Lemma
D.6

is the following, which states that events chosen for linearization
must have carried out some non-empty execution path.

Lemma D.7
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
If
x\in\overline{T}
then
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
.

Proof.

By definition of
\overline{T}
,
x\mathrel{{\prec}^{*}}y

for some
y\in T
.

•

Case
x=y
. Hence,
x\in T
. By Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::finished-operations-have-a-run}}
,

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
must hold.

•

Case
x\neq y
. Hence,
x\mathrel{{\prec}^{+}}y
.
By Lemma
D.6
,

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
must hold.

∎

The following lemma states that events ordered in real-time
have all their spans disjoint from each other, i.e., spans
do not go beyond the events they belong to.

Lemma D.8
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
If
{x}\sqsubset{y}
then
\forall b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x),b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(y).\ {b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.

Proof.

Let
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x),b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(y)
. By Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::blocks-contained-in-abstract-time-interval}}
,
we have
{y}.\textit{start}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{f({b_{y}})}.\textit{start}
and
there is
i
such that
{l({b_{x}})}\leq^{R}{i}
and

{i}.\textit{end}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{x}.\textit{end}
.

Hence, by definition of
\sqsubset
, we have,

{i}.\textit{end}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{x}.\textit{end}<_{\mathbb{N}}{y}.\textit{start}\leq_{\mathbb{N}}{f({b_{y}})}.\textit{start}

This means that
i
finishes in real-time before

f({b_{y}})
starts. But since
\leq^{R}
respects the real time order of rep events
(i.e.,
\leq^{R}
is a linearization),
we have
{i}<^{R}{f({b_{y}})}
.

But
{l({b_{x}})}\leq^{R}{i}
, which means

{l({b_{x}})}<^{R}{f({b_{y}})}
. Therefore,

{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.
∎

We can now prove each visibility axiom.

Lemma D.9
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::non-helpers}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{y}
and
z\in\mathcal{W}_{p}\cap\overline{T}
.
By definition of
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{y}
,
we need to consider two cases,

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{y}
.
By definition, there are

b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x)
and
b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(y)
such that

b_{x}
is the maximum satisfying

b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
and

{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.

From hypothesis
z\in\overline{T}
and Lemma
D.7
,

\mathcal{S}(z)\neq\emptyset
holds. So, by
Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::writers-have-writer-blocks}}
,
there is
b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}(z)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.

But
b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
and
b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
,
which means that either
{b_{y}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}
or

{b_{z}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
or
b_{y}=b_{z}
by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
.

–

Case
{b_{y}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}
. By definition,

{y}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{z}
holds
(So,
{y}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{z}
).

–

Case
{b_{z}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
.
Again, since
b_{x},b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
, we have three subcases by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
.

*

Case
{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}
.
We have

b_{x}\sqsubset^{S}b_{z}\sqsubset^{S}b_{y}
, which
is a contradiction, because
b_{x}
was the maximum satisfying
the conditions
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
and

{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{y}}
, but now
b_{z}
is a more recent
span satisfying the conditions.

*

Case
{b_{z}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}
. By definition,

{z}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{x}
holds
(So,
{z}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{x}
).

*

Case
b_{x}=b_{z}
. We have
\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}(z)\neq\emptyset
, which means
x=z
by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::runs-are-injective}}
.

–

Case
b_{y}=b_{z}
. We have
\mathcal{S}(y)\cap\mathcal{S}(z)\neq\emptyset
, which means
y=z
by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::runs-are-injective}}
.

•

Case
{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{y}
.
By definition, there are

b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x)
and
b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(y)
such that

b_{x}
is the maximum under
\sqsubseteq^{S}
satisfying

b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
,

{l({b_{x}})}<^{R}{l({b_{y}})}
, and
{f({b_{x}})}<^{R}{f({b_{y}})}
.

From hypothesis
z\in\overline{T}
and Lemma
D.7
,

\mathcal{S}(z)\neq\emptyset
holds. So, by
Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::writers-have-writer-blocks}}
,
there is
b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}(z)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.

Since
b_{x},b_{z}\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
, we have three cases by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
.

–

Case
{b_{x}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{z}}
.
Since rep events are totally ordered,
{l({b_{y}})}\leq^{R}{l({b_{z}})}
or

{l({b_{z}})}<^{R}{l({b_{y}})}
.

*

Case
{l({b_{y}})}\leq^{R}{l({b_{z}})}
.
Again, we have the following cases by the total order on rep events,

·

Case
{f({b_{z}})}\leq^{R}{l({b_{y}})}
.
Hence,
{f({b_{z}})}\leq^{R}{l({b_{y}})}\leq^{R}{l({b_{z}})}
,
which means that
\mathsf{OpAcc}\ {b_{y}}\ {z}
holds by definition.
Therefore,
{l({b_{y}})}<^{R}{l({b_{z}})}
by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::opportunistic-access}}
.
But this means
{y}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{z}
by definition
(So,
{y}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{z}
).

·

Case
{l({b_{y}})}<^{R}{f({b_{z}})}
. By Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
,

{f({b_{z}})}\leq^{R}{l({b_{z}})}
.
Hence,
{l({b_{y}})}<^{R}{l({b_{z}})}
,
which means

{l({b_{y}})}<^{R}{l({b_{z}})}
.
Therefore,
{y}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{z}
holds by definition
(So,
{y}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{z}
).

*

Case
{l({b_{z}})}<^{R}{l({b_{y}})}
.
Since rep events are totally ordered, we have
{f({b_{z}})}<^{R}{f({b_{y}})}

or
{f({b_{y}})}<^{R}{f({b_{z}})}
or
f({b_{y}})=f({b_{z}})
.

·

Case
{f({b_{z}})}<^{R}{f({b_{y}})}
.
We have

b_{x}\sqsubset^{S}b_{z}
and
l({b_{z}})<^{R}l({b_{y}})
by hypothesis, which
is a contradiction, because
b_{x}
was the maximum under
\sqsubseteq^{S}
satisfying
the conditions
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
and

{l({b_{x}})}<^{R}{l({b_{y}})}
and
{f({b_{x}})}<^{R}{f({b_{y}})}
,
but now
b_{z}
is a more recent
span under
\sqsubseteq^{S}
satisfying the conditions.

·

Case
{f({b_{y}})}<^{R}{f({b_{z}})}
. By definition,

{y}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{z}
holds
(So,
{y}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{z}
).

·

Case
f({b_{y}})=f({b_{z}})
. This contradicts Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::different-span-kinds-do-not-share-reps}}
.

–

Case
{b_{z}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}
. By definition,

{z}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{x}
holds
(So,
{z}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{x}
).

–

Case
b_{x}=b_{z}
. We have
\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}(z)\neq\emptyset
, which means
x=z
by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::runs-are-injective}}
.

∎

Lemma D.10
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::helped-are-writers}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
x,y\in\overline{T}
, and

{x}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}{y}
.

By definition of
\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}
, in either case

\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}
or

\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}
,
there is
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x)
such that
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.
So,
x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::writers-have-writer-blocks}}
.
∎

Lemma D.11
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::vis-acyclic}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
{x}\prec^{+}{y}
.
For a contradiction, suppose
{y}\sqsubseteq{x}
.
From
{y}\sqsubseteq{x}
, we have two cases,

•

Case
{y}\sqsubset{x}
.

By Lemma
D.6
, we have

{f({b_{x}})}<^{R}{l({b_{y}})}
for some
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x)
and

b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}(y)
.
But by Lemma

D.8
,
we also have
{b_{y}}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}
.
Hence, we have the contradiction,

f({b_{x}})<^{R}l({b_{y}})<^{R}f({b_{x}})

•

Case
y=x
. From
{x}\prec^{+}{y}
and Lemma

D.6

we have
x\neq y
(Contradiction).

∎

Lemma D.12
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::fin-predicate}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
x\in\overline{T}
. By Lemma
D.7
,

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
holds. Hence, by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::postcondition-predicate-holds}}
,

o({x})\neq\bot
and
\mathcal{Q}_{{x},{o({x})}}
hold.
In addition, if
x\in T
,
then Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::finished-operations-have-a-run}}
implies

o({x})={x}.\textit{out}
.
∎

Lemma D.13
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::allocs}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
x,y\in\mathcal{A}_{p}\cap\overline{T}
.
By Lemma
D.7
,

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}(y)\neq\emptyset
hold.

By Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::allocs-have-alloc-blocks}}
,
there are
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}\cap\mathcal{S}(x)
and

b_{y}\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}\cap\mathcal{S}(y)
.
But then
b_{x}=b_{y}
by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::containment-and-uniqueness-of-alloc-blocks}}
,
which implies
x=y
by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::runs-are-injective}}
.
∎

Lemma D.14
.

Suppose hypotheses
D.7
.
Axiom
C_{\ref{help-focused::all-writers-are-willing-helpers}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
x\in\mathcal{W}_{p}\cap\overline{T}
.

By Lemma
D.7
,

\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
holds. Hence, by Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::writers-have-writer-blocks}}
,
there is
b_{x}\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.
But then by Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::every-block-must-have-an-allocated-pointer}}

there is
b\in\mathcal{S}^{A}_{p}

such that
{b}\sqsubseteq^{S}{b_{x}}
.

•

Case
b=b_{x}
. By Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::allocs-have-alloc-blocks}}
we have

x\in\mathcal{A}_{p}
, and trivially

{x}\mathrel{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\phantom{$\ltimes$}}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$\ltimes$}\vskip-1.50696pt\hbox{$-$}}}}_{{p}}{x}
.

•

Case
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}
.
By Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::writer-blocks-belong-to-runs}}
,

b\in\mathcal{S}(z)
for some
z
.
By Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::allocs-have-alloc-blocks}}
we have

z\in\mathcal{A}_{p}
.
But since
{b}\sqsubset^{S}{b_{x}}
, we have

{z}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{x}
by definition
(So,
{z}\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}}{x}
).

∎

Theorem D.1
.

If hypotheses
D.7
hold, then

V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})

is valid.

Proof.

All visibility axioms hold by Lemmas from
D.9
to

D.14
.
∎

D.3
Proof of Opportunism Axioms

The full pseudocode for MCAS with opportunistic readers (or simply opportunistic MCAS)
is shown in
Figure
17
. MCAS uses the exportable
procedures of RDCSS as primitives.
The exportable procedures for MCAS are
mcas
,
mread
,

mwrite
, and
malloc
.

Val
denotes the set of all
possible input values.
Val
contains
neither MCAS descriptors nor pointers storing MCAS descriptors.

No particular implementation is provided for boolean predicate

\textit{is\_mdesc}(p)
, but it is assumed that it returns
true if and only if
p
is a pointer storing an MCAS descriptor.
For example,
[
11
]
suggests that
\textit{is\_mdesc}(p)

could be implemented by checking a reserved bit in
p
; this
reserved bit indicates whether or not the pointer stores a descriptor.

The implementation makes the following assumptions:

•

The list of entries given as input to procedure
mcas

is not empty.

•

The alloc at line
37

creates a pointer
d
such that
\textit{is\_mdesc}(d)
returns true.

•

Any input pointer
p:\textsc{pts}
to any
exportable procedure must satisfy
\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}(p)
.

•

Any input pointer to any procedure must have been previously
allocated with an invocation to
malloc
.

•

The alloc at line
33

returns a pointer
p
such that
\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}(p)
.

1:

record

update_entry
:

2:

pt
:
dptr

3:

exp
,
new
:
Val

4:

enum

status
:
Undec
,
Succ
,
Fail

5:

record

mdesc
:

6:

status
:
cptr

status

7:

entries
:
list

update_entry

8:

9:

proc

mread
(
pt:\textsc{dptr}
)

10:

old\leftarrow\textit{rread}(pt)

11:

if

\textit{is\_mdesc}(old)

then

12:

d\leftarrow\textit{rread}(old)

13:

s\leftarrow\textit{rread-c}({d}.\textit{status})

14:

e\leftarrow
entry for
pt
in
{d}.\textit{entries}

15:

if

s=\textsc{Succ}

then

16:

return
{e}.new

17:

else

18:

return
{e}.exp

19:

else

20:

return
old

21:

22:

proc

mwrite
(
pt:\textsc{dptr}
,
v:\textsc{Val}
)

23:

old\leftarrow\textit{rread}(pt)

24:

if

\textit{is\_mdesc}(old)

then

25:

\textit{mcas}^{\prime}(old)

26:

\textit{mwrite}(pt,v)

27:

else

28:

x\leftarrow\textit{rCAS}(pt,old,v)

29:

if

x\neq old

then

30:

\textit{mwrite}(pt,v)

(
v:\textsc{Val}
)

\textit{ralloc}(v,\textsc{dptr})

(
\overline{u}:\textsc{list}\,\textsc{update\_entry}
)

s\leftarrow\textit{ralloc}(\textsc{Undec},\textsc{cptr})

36:

desc\leftarrow\textsc{mdesc}(s,\overline{u})

37:

d\leftarrow\textit{ralloc}(desc,\textsc{dptr})

return
\textit{mcas}^{\prime}(d)

39:

proc

\textit{mcas}^{\prime}
(
d:\textsc{dptr}
)

41:

desc\leftarrow\textit{rread}(d)

42:

phase1\leftarrow\textit{rread-c}({desc}.\textit{status})

43:

if

phase1=\textsc{Undec}

then

44:

s\leftarrow\textit{writeall}(d,desc)

45:

\textit{rCAS-c}({desc}.\textit{status},\textsc{Undec},s)

46:

phase2\leftarrow\textit{rread-c}({desc}.\textit{status})

47:

r\leftarrow(phase2=\textsc{Succ})

48:

for each

e

in

{desc}.\textit{entries}
:

49:

\textit{rCAS}({e}.pt,d,r\ ?\ {e}.new:{e}.exp)

50:

return
r

51:

52:

proc

writeall
(
d:\textsc{dptr},desc:\textsc{mdesc}
)

53:

for each

e

in

{desc}.\textit{entries}
:

54:

rD\leftarrow\textsc{rdesc}({desc}.\textit{status},

55:

\phantom{rD\leftarrow\quad}{e}.pt,\textsc{Undec},{e}.exp,d)

56:

old\leftarrow\textit{rdcss}(rD)

57:

if

\textit{is\_mdesc}(old)

then

58:

if

old\neq d

then

59:

\textit{mcas}^{\prime}(old)

60:

return
\textit{writeall}(d,desc)

61:

else

if

then

62:

return
Fail

63:

return
Succ

Figure 17
:
Opportunistic MCAS implementation. It uses as primitives the exportable procedures in RDCSS.
Notice the opportunistic implementation of
mread
. All the other procedures are implemented as
in the helping MCAS implementation of Figure
14
.

We now define the opportunistic structure for opportunistic MCAS.

Definition D.9

(Opportunistic Structure for Opportunistic MCAS)
.

We denote the structure by
O^{\textsc{M}}
.

A span is either a 2-tuple of the form
(a,b)
or
a 1-tuple of the form
(a)
, where
a,b
are rep events in the execution history.

For each pointer
p
, set
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
is defined by the
following list of spans containing rep events,

1.

Any
(b)
such that,

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::invoke-rdcss-in-Oport-MCAS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b=\textit{rdcss}(desc)
for some
exp
and
desc=\textsc{rdesc}(\_,p,\textsc{Undec},exp,\_)

such that
{b}.\textit{out}\neq exp

2.

Any
(b,c)
such that,

•

{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::invoke-rdcss-in-Oport-MCAS}

•

{c}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::resolve-status-Oport-MCAS}

•

\neg\textit{is\_mdesc}({b}.\textit{out})

•

b
executes before
c
and there is no other
p
-write rep event between
b
and
c
.

•

b=\textit{rdcss}(desc)
and
c=\textit{rCAS}(s,\textsc{Undec},\textsc{Succ})
,
for some
s
,
exp
,
d
, and
desc=\textsc{rdesc}(s,p,\textsc{Undec},exp,d)
such that

c
is successful,
{b}.\textit{out}=exp
, and
\textit{is\_mdesc}(d)
.

1.

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\{(b)\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}\mid b\text{ writes or allocs pointer }p\}\cup\{(b,c)\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}\mid c=\textit{rCAS}(\_,\textsc{Undec},\textsc{Succ})\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}\{(b)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}\mid b\text{ allocs pointer }p\wedge{b}.\textit{line}=\ref{appendix::alloc-data-Alloc-Oport-MCAS}\}
