Notice how line
10

appears in both the definition of
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}

and
\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}
. When the line returns
a non-descriptor value, it is part of a d-span in

\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
, but when the line returns a
descriptor, it is part of an o-span in
\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}
.

We argue that
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
is well-defined.
For that matter, we need to show that the
function’s conditions pick spans uniquely.

The
mcas
,
mwrite
, and
malloc

cases are identical as in Definition
C.2

for the helping MCAS, the only difference is that we now use
set
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
instead of
\mathcal{S}_{p}
.
So, let us focus on
mread
, which is the only entry
that changed.

Case (2).(a) is choosing a rep event at line

10
and
such that its output does not satisfy

is_mdesc
(by definition of set
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
).
But this condition can happen at most once during the
invocation of
x
,
because the invoking thread finishes
x
once
the condition is satisfied.

Cases (2).(b) and (2).(c) are choosing an o-span
containing two rep events that execute exactly once
within the invocation of
x

(since in the opportunistic implementation
there are no loops or recursive calls).

We are ready to prove that

V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})
is implemented by opportunistic
structure
O^{\textsc{M}}
, i.e., we are going to prove that
the opportunism axioms of Figure
16
are satisfied. We require a couple of lemmas.

Lemma D.15
.

If
i_{1}
,
i_{2}
are allocs at line
33

such that they allocate the same pointer, then
i_{1}=i_{2}
.

Proof.

Identical to the proof of Lemma
C.17
.
∎

For the next lemma we make the following definition,

Definition D.10
.

We say that
v\in\textsc{Val}
is the value that
p
is bound to by
(a,\_)\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}

if there are
rD
,
desc
,
j
such that,

•

a=\textit{rdcss}(rD)
,

•

{rD}.new_{2}
was allocated by rep event
\textit{ralloc}(desc,\textsc{dptr})
at line

37
,

•

j\in{desc}.\textit{entries}
,

•

p={rD}.pt_{2}={pt}_{j}
,

•

v={new}_{j}
.

Alternatively, one can think of
v
is the new value that
line
49
will write into
p

because span
(a,\_)
has already resolved the descriptor status to
Succ
.

Lemma D.16
.

If
b\in\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
,
then there is
x
such that
b\in\mathcal{S}(x)
and
\textsc{writer}\ {x}\ {p}\ {v}
,
where
v
is the value determined by one of the following cases:

•

If
b
is of the form
(i)
for some rep
i
, then
v
is the value written by
i
.

•

If
b
is of the form
(a,\_)
for some rep
a
, then

v
is the value that
p
is bound to by
b
.

Proof.

Identical to the proof of Lemma
C.18
.
∎

Lemma D.17
.

We have the following propositions.

1.

Suppose
x
is one of
mcas
, or
mread
. Suppose
p\in\textsc{pts}
and
v\in\textsc{Val}
. Then,
the following statements are equivalent,

•

There is
y
such that
{y}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{D}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {y}\ {p}\ {v}
.

•

There is
b\in\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}(x)
such that
f({b})
reads value
v
.

2.

Suppose
x
is
mread
. If
(a,b)\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(x)
and
{b}.\textit{out}=\textsc{Succ}
, then
there is
y
such that
{y}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {y}\ {p}\ {{new}_{j}}
for some
desc
and
j\in{desc}.\textit{entries}
such that

p={pt}_{j}
and
desc
is the descriptor discovered by
a
.

3.

Suppose
x
is
mread
. If
(a,b)\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}(x)
and
{b}.\textit{out}\neq\textsc{Succ}
, then
there is
y
such that
{y}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}}^{\mathsf{O}}{x}

and
\textsc{writer}\ {y}\ {p}\ {{exp}_{j}}
for some
desc
and
j\in{desc}.\textit{entries}
such that

p={pt}_{j}
and
desc
is the descriptor discovered by
a
.

Proof.

We prove each proposition.

1.

Identical to the proof of Lemma
C.19
,
but using
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
instead of
\mathcal{S}_{p}
.

2.

Since
a
discovers a descriptor
desc
and
b
finds the descriptor status
to have succeeded (
{b}.\textit{out}=\textsc{Succ}
),
it means that
desc
must have been written and resolved by a
span
(g,h)
such that
g<^{R}a
and
h<^{R}b
.
Also,
p
must be one of the input pointers in
{desc}.\textit{entries}
, i.e.,

p={pt}_{j}
for some
j\in{desc}.\textit{entries}
, otherwise,

a
would not have found a descriptor in
p
in the first place.

The successful span
(g,h)
must be the most recent one satisfying

g<^{R}a
and
h<^{R}b

because if there is a more recent successful writer span, it will have to occur
after
a
, since the descriptor was still present when
a
discovered it.
Notice that
{new}_{j}
is the value
p={pt}_{j}
is bound to by
(g,h)
,
since line
49
will eventually write
{new}_{j}
into
p
as

h
marked the descriptor as
succeeded. The result follows by Lemma
D.16
.

3.

Since
a
discovers a descriptor
desc
, it means that
desc
must have been written by a
rep event
g
such that
g<^{R}a
.
Also,
p
must be one of the input pointers in
{desc}.\textit{entries}
, i.e.,

p={pt}_{j}
for some
j\in{desc}.\textit{entries}
, otherwise,

a
would not have found a descriptor in
p
in the first place.

•

If
{b}.\textit{out}=\textsc{Fail}
, then
g
must be part of a span
(g,h)
, where
h

must have resolved the descriptor to
Fail
before
b

discovered the status, i.e.,
(g,h)
is not a successful span.
Rep event
g
must have found value
{exp}_{j}
in
p={pt}_{j}
,
otherwise it would not have written the descriptor in the first place (line
56
).
This means that, before rep event
g
, there must exist a recent successful span
(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})
that
either wrote value
{exp}_{j}

or had
{exp}_{j}
as bound value that later got written by line
49
.
Since
(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})
finishes before
g
, we have
g^{\prime}<^{R}a
and

h^{\prime}<^{R}b
, and
(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})
is the most recent successful span
satisfying those conditions.
The result follows by Lemma
D.16
.

•

If
{b}.\textit{out}=\textsc{Undec}
, then
desc
has not been resolved yet,
which means that
g
is still not part of a span (or if
g
is part of a span, such span ends after
b
).
Rep event
g
must have found value
{exp}_{j}
in
p={pt}_{j}
,
otherwise it would not have written the descriptor in the first place (line
56
).
This means that, before rep event
g
, there must exist a recent successful span
(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})
that
either wrote value
{exp}_{j}

or had
{exp}_{j}
as bound value that later got written by line
49
.
Since
(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})
finishes before
g
, we have
g^{\prime}<^{R}a
and

h^{\prime}<^{R}b
, and
(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})
is the most recent successful span
satisfying those conditions (in case
g
is part of a span, such span does not satisfy the condition
of finalizing before
b
since
desc
is still
Undec
).
The result follows by Lemma
D.16
.

∎

Lemma D.18
.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptors-do-not-interfere}}
holds.

Proof.

Identical to the proof in Lemma
C.20
,
but using
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
instead of
\mathcal{S}_{p}
.
∎

Lemma D.19
.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::all-descriptors-are-written-before-any-resolution}}
holds.

Proof.

This is trivial when
x
has a denotation with only one span. And the cases for

x=\textit{mcas}(\overline{u})
are identical to the proof of Lemma

C.21
.
∎

Lemma D.20
.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::opportunistic-access}}
holds.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathsf{OpAcc}\ {b}\ {x}
, which means
b\in\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}

and
f({c})\leq^{R}l({b})\leq^{R}l({c})

for some
p
and
c\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{p}
.

c
cannot be a 1-span because we would have
l({b})=f({c})=l({c})
, contradicting
that d-spans and o-spans do not share rep events. So,
c
must have the form
(d,e)
,
where
d
writes the descriptor
desc
into
p
and
e
sets the descriptor status to
Succ
.
This means that the denotation of
x
must have the form
\{(a_{j},e)\mid j\in{desc}.\textit{entries}\}
, since
it is the only case that contains a span like
c=(d,e)
.
Since
l({b})\leq^{R}l({c})=e
, then
l({b})\leq^{R}l({(a_{j},e)})=e

for every
j
, which proves the axiom.
∎

Lemma D.21
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::finished-operations-have-a-run}}
holds.

Proof.

The cases for
mcas
,
mwrite
and
malloc
are identical to the cases
as in Lemma
C.22
, since their code is identical.
Hence, we only need to check the
mread
method.

Suppose that
\textit{mread}(p)
finished.
Let
T
be the invoking thread.
If
T
reached line 20, then the read at line 10 returned a
non-descriptor value which is returned by
mread
. This corresponds to
(2).(a) in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
with output whatever
line 10 produced and the denotation is not empty.
If
T
reached line 16, then the
read at line 10 returned a descriptor. The status read at line 13 must
have returned
Succ
, and the method returned
new_{p}
corresponding to
the new value for pointer
p
in the descriptor. This corresponds to
(2).(b) in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
with output
new_{p}
and
the denotation is not empty.
If
T
reached line 18, then the read at line 10
returned a descriptor. The status read at line 13 must have returned
either
Undec
or
Fail
, and the method returned
exp_{p}
corresponding to
the expected value for pointer
p
in the descriptor. This corresponds
to (2).(c) in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
with output
exp_{p}

and the denotation is not empty.
∎

Lemma D.22
.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-write-precedes-resolution}}
holds.

Proof.

Trivial for 1-spans. For 2-tuple d-spans
(a,b)
,
the code can only resolve descriptors (rep event
b
)
if the descriptor was previously written at line
51

(rep event
a
).
For 2-tuple o-spans, the code can only look into
the descriptor status (rep event
b
) if the the read
at line
10
discovered a descriptor (rep event
a
).
∎

Lemma D.23
.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::descriptor-opor-spans-disjoint}}
holds.

Proof.

The only possible cases where there could be an overlap between a d-span and an o-span
is in cases (4) of
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
and (1) of
\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}
. However,
in (4), the rep event at line
10
is required to return a
non-descriptor value, while in (1), the rep event at line
10

is required to return a descriptor. Therefore, the spans are different because they contain
different rep events.
∎

Lemma D.24
.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::spans-access-at-most-one-pointer}}
holds.

Proof.

All spans
b
in
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
and
\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}
are defined so that
the pointer they access is determined by the pointer that
f({b})
accesses.
All rep events access at most one pointer, with the exception of the
rdcss
at line

56
. However, looking at cases (1), (2), (3) of

\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
, we see that the spans define
pt_{2}
in the descriptor as the
pointer accessed by
f({b})
.

So, if a span
b
accesses pointers
p
and
q
, then
f({b})
accesses
p
and
q
, which
means
p=q
.
∎

Lemma D.25
.

Axiom
O_{\ref{opor::containment-and-uniqueness-of-alloc-blocks}}
holds.

Proof.

Identical to the proof of Lemma
C.29
.
∎

Lemma D.26
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::every-block-must-have-an-allocated-pointer}}
holds.

Proof.

Identical to the proof of Lemma
C.30
.
∎

Lemma D.27
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::runs-are-injective}}
holds.

Proof.

First, when neither
x
nor
y
are
mread
events, the reasoning
is identical as in the proof of Lemma
C.24
.
So, we can assume that either
x
is a
mread
event or
y
is.
Say,
x
is a
mread
event.

All the spans in the cases for
mread
satisfy that
f({b})
has code line

10
and it is executed by the same thread
that invoked
x
and it occurs within the invocation of
x
.
Since
y
also includes
b
, event
y
must also be a
mread

(other events do not have denotations containing line
10
),
which means that
x
and
y
must be invoked by the same thread and
b
executes within the
invocation of both
x
and
y
.

If
x\neq y
, then
x
and
y
cannot overlap in real-time, because they
are invoked by the same thread. But this contradicts that
b
occurs within
the invocation of both
x
and
y
. Therefore,
x=y
.
∎

Lemma D.28
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::writer-blocks-belong-to-runs}}
holds.

Proof.

Directly from Lemma
D.16
.
∎

Lemma D.29
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::postcondition-predicate-holds}}
holds.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
. We see from definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket
that all non-empty cases have
o({x})\neq\bot
.
To prove
\mathcal{Q}_{{x},{o({x})}}
, we do a case analysis on
x
.

•

Case
x=\textit{mcas}(\overline{u})
.
Identical to the
\textit{mcas}(\overline{u})
case in the proof for Lemma
C.26
, but
using Lemma
D.17
to conclude that in the
o({x})=true
case,
for every
i\in\overline{u}
,
there is
z
such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}^{\mathsf{D}}{x}
(hence

{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}{x}
)
and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {{pt}_{i}}\ {{exp}_{i}}
; and in the
o({x})=false
case,
for some
i\in\overline{u}
,
there is
z
such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}^{\mathsf{D}}{x}
(hence

{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}{x}
)
and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {{pt}_{i}}\ {v}
for some
v\neq{{exp}_{i}}
.

•

The cases
x=\textit{malloc}(v)
and
x=\textit{mwrite}(q,v)
are identical as in the proof for
Lemma
C.26
.

•

Case
x=\textit{rread}(q)
. We want to prove,

\displaystyle\exists z.\ {z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}{x}\wedge\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {q}\ {o({x})}

Each case in the definition of
\left\llbracket{x}\right\rrbracket

has a span
b
such that
f({b})
reads a descriptor or a non-descriptor value in
q
at line
10
.

If
f({b})
reads a non-descriptor value, then
b
is a d-span.
Therefore, by Lemma
D.17
,
there is
z
such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}^{\mathsf{D}}{x}

(hence
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}{x}
)
and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {q}\ {o({x})}
.

If
f({b})
reads a descriptor
desc
, then
b
is an o-span, and
q={pt}_{j}
for some

j\in{desc}.\textit{entries}
.

–

Case
{l({b})}.\textit{out}=\textsc{Succ}
.
Then
o({x})={new}_{j}

and by Lemma
D.17
, there is
z

such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}^{\mathsf{O}}{x}

(hence
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}{x}
)
and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {q}\ {o({x})}
.

–

Case
{l({b})}.\textit{out}\neq\textsc{Succ}
.
Then
o({x})={exp}_{j}

and by Lemma
D.17
, there is
z

such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}^{\mathsf{O}}{x}

(hence
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{q}}{x}
)
and
\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {q}\ {o({x})}
.

∎

Lemma D.30
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::different-span-kinds-do-not-share-reps}}
holds.

Proof.

By definition of the d-spans and o-spans, they do not share rep events.
For example, spans in
\mathcal{S}^{O}_{p}
have as first rep event
the read at line
10
that always
returns a descriptor. While d-spans in
\mathcal{S}^{D}_{p}
never return
a descriptor in their first rep event.
∎

Lemma D.31
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::runs-have-at-most-one-type-of-span}}
holds.

Proof.

An inspection of the cases in
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
shows that d-spans and o-spans
do not get mixed in the output sets.
∎

Lemma D.32
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::opportunism-is-unique}}
holds.

Proof.

mread
is the only event in the definition of
\left\llbracket{\cdot}\right\rrbracket
that can produce o-spans.
Notice that the cases for
mread
produce only one o-span. Therefore,
any two o-spans in the denotation of an
mread
event will be equal.
∎

Lemma D.33
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::writers-have-writer-blocks}}
holds.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathcal{S}(x)\neq\emptyset
. We do a case analysis on
x
.

•

Case
x=\textit{mcas}(\overline{u})
.

\Longrightarrow
. By definition of
\mathcal{W}_{p}
, we have
p={pt}_{j}
for some
j\in\overline{u}
and,

\displaystyle\forall i\in\overline{u}.\ \exists z.\ {z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}{x}\wedge\textsc{writer}\ {z}\ {{pt}_{i}}\ {{exp}_{i}}

(27)

Hence, for every
i\in\overline{u}
, there is
z
such that
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}^{\mathsf{D}}{x}
or

{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}^{\mathsf{O}}{x}
.
If
{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}^{\mathsf{O}}{x}
holds,
then
x
would have o-spans in its denotation, which is impossible. So, we can assume

{z}\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{{pt}_{i}}}^{\mathsf{D}}{x}
.

By Lemma
D.17
, for every
i\in\overline{u}
, there are

b_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{{pt}_{i}}(x)
such that

f({b_{i}})
reads value
{exp}_{i}
.

The only case in the denotation that matches this conditions is (1).(a), i.e.,
the successful denotation. In particular, for
j\in\overline{u}
,

b_{j}\in\mathcal{S}(x)\cap\mathcal{S}^{W}_{{pt}_{j}}
.

\Longleftarrow
. Identical to the proof of Lemma

C.27
, but using
Lemma
D.17
instead.

•

The cases for
x=\textit{malloc}(v)
,
x=\textit{mwrite}(q,v)
, and

x=\textit{mread}(q)
are similar to the proof of
Lemma
C.27
.

∎

Lemma D.34
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::allocs-have-alloc-blocks}}
holds.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to the proof of Lemma
C.31
,
but using code line
33
.
∎

Lemma D.35
.

Axiom

O_{\ref{opor::blocks-contained-in-abstract-time-interval}}
holds.

Proof.

We prove each item.

•

(i).
Let us focus on the
mread
case, since the rest of cases are as
in the proof of Lemma
C.28
.

All the three cases for
mread
contain a single span
b
such that
f({b})
is
executed by the same thread that invoked
x
. Therefore,
b
starts after
x
starts.

•

(ii).
Let us focus on the
mread
case, since the rest of cases are as
in the proof of Lemma
C.28
.

All the three cases for
mread
contain a single span
b
such that
l({b})
is
executed by the same thread that invoked
x
. Therefore, we can choose
i\mathrel{\>\widehat{=}\>}l({b})
,
since it is guarantied that it will finish before
x
finishes.

∎

Theorem D.2
.

V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})

is implemented by
opportunistic structure
O^{\textsc{M}}
.

Proof.

All opportunism axioms hold from Lemma
D.18
to
Lemma
D.35
.
∎

Theorem D.3
.

The MCAS implementation of Figure
17
is linearizable.

Proof.

By Theorem
A.1
, it
suffices to show that

V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})
is valid. But by
Theorem
D.1
, it suffices that

V^{\textsc{M}}(\mathrel{\lessdot}_{{p}},\mathrel{\ltimes}_{{p}})
is implemented by opportunistic
structure
O^{\textsc{M}}
. This is given by Theorem

D.2
.
∎
